# Same Count, Different History: Hidden-Height Operators, Shifted Legendre Horizons, and Euler–Bernoulli Cantilever Spectra in Permutation Random Walks

**Status:** *Research in Progress (`v0.1-wip` · Working Paper for GitHub ↔ Zenodo)*

## Abstract

Following Wenpin Tang (*arXiv:2609.25102v1*), let $\pi = (\pi_0, \pi_1, \dots, \pi_m)$ be a uniform random permutation of $\{1, \dots, m+1\}$, let $X_i = \mathbf{1}_{\{\pi_i > \pi_{i-1}\}} - \mathbf{1}_{\{\pi_i < \pi_{i-1}\}} \in \{\pm 1\}$ be its up-down step signs, and let $S_t = \sum_{i=1}^t X_i$ be the associated discrete height walk. Because $S_{2k-1}$ is always odd and nonzero, the cumulative count of positive odd heights $A_n = \sum_{k=1}^n \mathbf{1}_{\{S_{2k-1} > 0\}}$ is uniformly distributed on $\{0, 1, \dots, n\}$ and matches classical Pólya$(1, 1)$ urn statistics in all one-dimensional and adjacent-pair marginals $(A_{n-1}, A_n)$. Yet the binary indicator process $I_k = \mathbf{1}_{\{S_{2k-1} > 0\}}$ is strictly non-exchangeable and non-Markovian.

In this working paper (*Research in Progress*), we establish five exact analytical and combinatorial results characterizing how the sign process remembers its past and how sign switches reset that memory:
1. **Direct Combinatorial Splitting via MacMahon's Ribbon Theorem (§2):** For $A_2 = 1$, the history `+-` pins $S_3 = -1$ and restricts `+-+` to a single 5-step descent signature $(+, -, -, +, +)$ ($D = \{2, 3\}$ in $\mathfrak{S}_6$), giving $\binom{6}{2,1,3} - \binom{6}{2,4} - \binom{6}{3,3} + \binom{6}{6} = 60 - 15 - 20 + 1 = 26$ permutations and $\mathbb{P}(+ \mid +-) = 13/45$, whereas `-+` pins $S_3 = +1$ and gives $\mathbb{P}(+ \mid -+) = 32/45$.
2. **Piecewise Differential-Difference Operator on Unit Intervals (§3):** In Tang's continuous triangular representation $T_k = \sum_{j=1}^k Y_j$ ($f_Y(y) = (1 - |y|)_+$), the unnormalized density $f_j(x) = f_k(j+x)$ ($x \in [0, 1]$) of $|T_k|$ satisfies:
   - **Sign switch ($\sigma_k \neq \sigma_{k-1}$):** Complete reset onto $j = 0$ ($[0, 1]$) via $(T f_0)(x) = \int_0^{1-x} (1 - x - t) f_0(t)\,dt$, i.e., $(f_0^{\text{new}})''(x) = f_0(1-x)$ with $f_0^{\text{new}}(1) = (f_0^{\text{new}})'(1) = 0$.
   - **Sign repeat ($\sigma_k = \sigma_{k-1}$):** Exact equivalence between continuous second derivatives and discrete second differences across unit intervals, $(f_j^{\text{new}})''(x) = f_{j-1}(x) - 2 f_j(x) + f_{j+1}(x)$.
3. **Exact $m$-Step Horizon via Shifted Legendre Projection $D = 2m$ (§4):** Orthogonal $L^2([0, 1])$ projection $\Pi_D$ of each unit piece $f_j(x)$ onto shifted Legendre polynomials $P_d^*(x) = P_d(2x-1)$ of degree $d \le D = 2m$ ($2m+1$ moments per unit interval) preserves the conditional probability of every $m$-step future sign continuation with **zero error ($\varepsilon = 0$)**.
4. **Euler–Bernoulli Cantilever Spectrum of Sign Switches (§5):** The compact self-adjoint turn operator $T = R\mathcal{I}^2$ on $L^2([0, 1])$ reduces under $g(u) = \phi(1-u)$ to the classical 4th-order **Euler–Bernoulli (1744) cantilever beam boundary value problem** $g^{(4)}(u) = \kappa^4 g(u)$, $g(0)=g'(0)=g''(1)=g'''(1)=0$, with characteristic equation $\cosh(\kappa_m)\cos(\kappa_m) = -1$ and eigenvalues $\lambda_m = (-1)^{m-1} / \kappa_m^2$ ($\lambda_1 \approx +0.284412871855$, $\lambda_2 \approx -0.045383393443$). Consequently, each sign switch compresses pre-turn memory by the asymptotic factor $\rho_{\text{turn}} = (\kappa_2/\kappa_1)^2 \approx \mathbf{6.266893025771}$, with alternating-sign oscillations caused by $\lambda_2 < 0 < \lambda_3$.
5. **Hierarchy of Forgotten Information, $O(r^{-2})$ Gap Decay, and Wiener–Hopf Limits (§6):** When two distinct prefixes are followed by $r$ identical signs, their one-step forecast gap decays as $O(r^{-2})$. For the 2-parameter summary $(\text{sign}, r)$, the Bayesian forecast at $r=1$ admits the exact Edgeworth expansion $\hat{p}_n(-, 1) = \frac{3}{10} - \frac{3}{175(n-1)} + O(n^{-2})$, while the extreme histories `'+'^{n-1} '-'` and `'-'^{n-2} '+-'` converge to Wiener–Hopf limits $P_{\max}(\infty) \approx 0.3151600752$ and $P_{\min}(\infty) \approx 0.2793320208$, bounding the worst-case error of $(\text{sign}, r)$ by $\frac{3}{10} - P_{\min}(\infty) \approx \mathbf{0.0206679792}$ as $n \to \infty$.

## 1. Setup and the Pólya-Urn Paradox

Let $\pi = (\pi_0, \pi_1, \dots, \pi_{2n})$ be a uniform random permutation of $\{1, \dots, 2n+1\}$, and let
$$X_i = \begin{cases} +1 & \text{if } \pi_i > \pi_{i-1}, \\ -1 & \text{if } \pi_i < \pi_{i-1}, \end{cases} \qquad S_t = \sum_{i=1}^t X_i.$$
At odd times $t = 2k-1$, the height $S_{2k-1}$ is an odd integer and therefore never zero. Let $\sigma_k = \operatorname{sgn}(S_{2k-1}) \in \{+, -\}$ denote the sign of the $k$-th odd height, and let $A_n = \sum_{k=1}^n \mathbf{1}_{\{\sigma_k = +\}}$ be the number of positive odd heights up to step $2n-1$.

By Tang (*arXiv:2609.25102v1*), the sequence of odd heights has the same law as the signs of $T_k = \sum_{j=1}^k Y_j$, where $Y_1, Y_2, \dots$ are i.i.d. symmetric triangular random variables on $(-1, 1)$ with density $f_Y(y) = 1 - |y|$. Moreover:
- $A_n \sim \operatorname{Uniform}\{0, 1, \dots, n\}$, matching the classical Pólya$(1, 1)$ urn;
- Every adjacent pair $(A_{n-1}, A_n)$ matches the Pólya$(1, 1)$ transition law $\mathbb{P}(A_n = a+1 \mid A_{n-1} = a) = \frac{a+1}{n+1}$.

However, conditioning on the ordered sign history $(\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_{n-1})$ rather than the scalar count $A_{n-1}$ breaks exchangeability starting at $n = 3$: for $A_2 = 1$, Pólya's urn predicts $\frac{1+1}{2+2} = \frac{1}{2}$, whereas
$$\mathbb{P}(\sigma_3 = + \mid \sigma_1\sigma_2 = \text{`+-`}) = \frac{13}{45} \approx 0.2889, \qquad \mathbb{P}(\sigma_3 = + \mid \sigma_1\sigma_2 = \text{`-+`}) = \frac{32}{45} \approx 0.7111.$$

---

## 2. Theorem 1: Direct Combinatorial Proof via MacMahon's Ribbon Theorem

**Lemma 2.1 (Sign-Switch Pinning at Odd Steps).** *Because $S_{2k+1} - S_{2k-1} = X_{2k} + X_{2k+1} \in \{-2, 0, +2\}$ while $S_{2k-1}$ is odd, any sign change between consecutive odd steps ($\sigma_k \neq \sigma_{k+1}$) forces $|S_{2k-1}| = |S_{2k+1}| = 1$. Specifically:*
- $\sigma_k \sigma_{k+1} = \text{`+-`} \implies S_{2k-1} = +1,\; X_{2k} = X_{2k+1} = -1,\; S_{2k+1} = -1$;
- $\sigma_k \sigma_{k+1} = \text{`-+`} \implies S_{2k-1} = -1,\; X_{2k} = X_{2k+1} = +1,\; S_{2k+1} = +1$.

**Theorem 2.2 (Combinatorial Derivation of $26/720 = 13/360$ and $13/45$ vs $32/45$).** *In $\mathfrak{S}_6$ ($6! = 720$ permutations of $\{1, \dots, 6\}$):*
1. *Every permutation with $\sigma_1\sigma_2 = \text{`+-`}$ has $(X_1, X_2, X_3) = (+1, -1, -1)$ and $S_3 = -1$ ($90$ permutations in total, since $\mathbb{P}(\text{`+-`}) = 1/8 = 90/720$).*
2. *To reach $S_5 > 0$ from $S_3 = -1$, the next two steps are uniquely forced to be $(X_4, X_5) = (+1, +1)$ ($S_5 = +1$). Thus the event $\sigma_1\sigma_2\sigma_3 = \text{`+-+`}$ consists of a single 5-step up-down signature $(+, -, -, +, +)$, i.e.,*
   $$\pi_0 < \pi_1 > \pi_2 > \pi_3 < \pi_4 < \pi_5 \quad (\text{descent set } D = \{2, 3\} \subset \{1, 2, 3, 4, 5\}).$$
3. *By MacMahon's ribbon inclusion-exclusion formula for the composition $(2, 1, 3)$ of $6$, the number of permutations in $\mathfrak{S}_6$ with descent set $D = \{2, 3\}$ is*
   $$N(\{2, 3\}) = \binom{6}{2, 1, 3} - \binom{6}{2, 4} - \binom{6}{3, 3} + \binom{6}{6} = 60 - 15 - 20 + 1 = \mathbf{26}.$$
4. *Consequently, $\mathbb{P}(\text{`+-+`}) = \frac{26}{720} = \frac{13}{360}$ and $\mathbb{P}(+ \mid \text{`+-`}) = \frac{26}{90} = \mathbf{\frac{13}{45}}$. By reflection symmetry $\pi_i \mapsto 7 - \pi_i$, $\mathbb{P}(- \mid \text{`-+`}) = \frac{13}{45}$ and therefore*
   $$\mathbb{P}(+ \mid \text{`-+`}) = 1 - \frac{13}{45} = \frac{64}{90} = \mathbf{\frac{32}{45}}.$$

*(Note: Those 64 permutations for `-++` split at $t=5$ into $59$ paths with $S_5 = +1$ and $5 = \binom{6}{1} - 1$ paths with signature $(-, +, +, +, +)$ and $S_5 = +3$.)*

---

## 3. Theorem 2: Piecewise Differential-Difference Operator for Hidden Height

In Tang's triangular representation $T_k = T_{k-1} + Y_k$ ($f_Y(y) = (1 - |y|)_+$ on $(-1, 1)$), let $U_k = |T_k| > 0$ be the continuous distance of $T_k$ from zero after a fixed sign history $\sigma = (\sigma_1, \dots, \sigma_k)$. Its unnormalized density is supported on $[0, r+1]$, where $r \ge 0$ is the number of consecutive repeats at the end of $\sigma$, and is piecewise polynomial of degree $2k-1$ on each unit interval:
$$f_j(x) = f_{U_k}(j + x), \qquad x \in [0, 1], \quad j = 0, 1, \dots, r.$$

**Theorem 3.1 (Reset on Sign Switch and Discrete-Continuous Identity on Repeat).**
1. **Sign Switch ($\sigma_{k+1} \neq \sigma_k$):** *Because $|Y_{k+1}| < 1$, a sign change is possible only from the boundary unit interval $j = 0$ ($U_k \in (0, 1)$). All higher pieces $j \ge 1$ are annihilated ($f_j^{\text{new}} \equiv 0$ for $j \ge 1$), and the new density on $[0, 1]$ is given by the reflected double-integration operator $T = R\mathcal{I}^2$:*
   $$f_0^{\text{new}}(x) = (T f_0)(x) = \int_0^{1-x} (1 - x - t)\,f_0(t)\,dt, \qquad (f_0^{\text{new}})''(x) = f_0(1 - x), \quad f_0^{\text{new}}(1) = (f_0^{\text{new}})'(1) = 0.$$
   *In particular, for the purely alternating sign sequence $+ - + - \dots$ of length $n$, the density $f_n(x)$ on $[0, 1]$ satisfies the boundary value problem $f_n''(x) = f_{n-1}(1-x)$ with $f_n(1)=f_n'(1)=0$ and $f_1(x) = 1 - x$, yielding exact probabilities $1/2,\; 1/8,\; 13/360,\; 59/5760,\; 661/226800,\; 12031/14515200$ ($397\,023 / 12!$) for $n = 1, \dots, 6$.*
2. **Sign Repeat ($\sigma_{k+1} = \sigma_k$):** *On every unit interval $[j, j+1]$ ($x \in (0, 1)$), the continuous second derivative of the updated piece equals the discrete second difference across adjacent unit intervals:*
   $$(f_j^{\text{new}})''(x) = f_{j-1}(x) - 2 f_j(x) + f_{j+1}(x).$$
3. **One-Step Forecast Functional:** *Given the pieces $\{f_j\}_{j=0}^r$ of total mass $M = \sum_{j=0}^r \int_0^1 f_j(x)\,dx$, the conditional probability that the next sign flips is determined solely by the quadratic moment on the boundary interval $j = 0$:*
   $$\mathbb{P}(\sigma_{k+1} \neq \sigma_k \mid \sigma) = \frac{1}{M}\int_0^1 \frac{1}{2}(1 - x)^2 f_0(x)\,dx.$$

---

## 4. Theorem 3: Exact $m$-Step Horizon via Shifted Legendre Projection $D = 2m$

Let $P_d^*(x) = P_d(2x - 1)$ ($d = 0, 1, \dots$) be the orthogonal shifted Legendre polynomials on $[0, 1]$, and let $\Pi_D : L^2([0, 1]) \to \mathbb{P}_D([0, 1])$ be the orthogonal projection onto polynomials of degree $\le D$ (storing $D+1$ moments per unit interval).

**Theorem 4.1 (Single-Shot Horizon $D = 2m$ and Streaming Horizon $n \le D$ with Unit-Fraction First Error).** *Because double integration maps $\mathcal{I}^2 P_d^* \in \operatorname{span}(P_{d-2}^*, P_d^*, P_{d+2}^*)$ for $d \ge 2$ (orthogonal to $\mathbb{P}_{d-3}$) while the reflection $R P_d^*(x) = P_d^*(1-x) = (-1)^d P_d^*(x)$ preserves degree, the adjoint of each step operator raises the polynomial degree of the test functional $\frac{1}{2}(1-x)^2 \in \mathbb{P}_2$ by at most $2$ per step. Consequently:*
1. **Single-Shot Horizon ($D = 2m$):** *Applying $\Pi_D$ once to an arbitrary density $f_k$ with $D = 2m$ ($2m+1$ numbers per unit interval) preserves the exact conditional probability of every $m$-step future sign continuation $(\sigma_{k+1}, \dots, \sigma_{k+m})$ with **zero error ($\varepsilon = 0$)**.*
2. **Streaming Horizon ($n \le D$) and Exact Unit-Fraction Identity at $n = D + 1$ (Petrovich, 2026-11-07):** *In recursive streaming compression $\tilde{f}_s = \Pi_D\mathcal{L}\tilde{f}_{s-1}$ starting from $f_1(x) = 1 - x$, the forecast error is **identically zero ($\varepsilon = 0$) for all trajectory lengths $n \le D$** ($n_{\text{exact}} = \lfloor(D+1)/2\rfloor + 1 + \lfloor(D-2)/2\rfloor = D$). At the first non-exact step $n = D + 1$ along the alternating trajectory, the denominator mass is still exact ($\tilde{M}_{D+1} = M_{D+1} = A_{D+1}/(2D+2)!$, where $A_{D+1}$ is the integer count of alternating-sign permutations in $S_{2D+2}$: $A_2=3, A_3=26, A_4=413, A_5=10\,576, A_6=397\,023, A_7=20\,551\,376$), while the numerator loses the single highest Legendre mode $P_{D+1}^*$ of weight $\Delta M_{D+2} = \bigl[2(2D+3)\binom{2D+2}{D+1}(2D+2)!\bigr]^{-1}$. Thus $(2D+2)!$ cancels identically, and the first non-zero streaming error is always a **unit fraction** for all $D \ge 1$:*
   $$p_{D+1}^{\text{exact}} - p_{D+1}^{\text{stream}} = \mathbf{\frac{1}{2(2D+3)\binom{2D+2}{D+1}\,A_{D+1}}} = \frac{1}{180},\; \frac{1}{7\,280},\; \frac{1}{520\,380},\; \frac{1}{58\,633\,344},\; \frac{1}{9\,538\,080\,552},\; \frac{1}{2\,115\,969\,672\,960} \quad (D=1,\dots,6).$$

---

## 5. Theorem 4: Euler–Bernoulli Cantilever Beam Spectrum of Sign Switches

**Theorem 5.1 (Cantilever Eigenvalue Problem for $T = R\mathcal{I}^2$).** *The sign-switch operator $(T f)(x) = \int_0^{1-x} (1 - x - t) f(t)\,dt$ has symmetric kernel $K(x, t) = (1 - x - t)_+$ and is compact and self-adjoint on $L^2([0, 1])$. For any eigenfunction $T \phi = \lambda \phi$, setting $u = 1 - x$ and $g(u) = \phi(1 - u)$ yields $g''(u) = \lambda^{-1} g(1 - u)$ and hence the 4th-order boundary value problem*
$$g^{(4)}(u) = \kappa^4 g(u) \quad \bigl(\kappa = |\lambda|^{-1/2}\bigr), \qquad g(0) = g'(0) = 0, \quad g''(1) = g'''(1) = 0.$$
*This is identical to the classical **Euler–Bernoulli (1744) vibrating cantilever beam** clamped at $u = 0$ ($x = 1$) and free at $u = 1$ ($x = 0$). Its roots $\kappa_1 < \kappa_2 < \kappa_3 < \dots$ satisfy*
$$\cosh(\kappa_m)\cos(\kappa_m) = -1, \qquad \lambda_m = \frac{(-1)^{m-1}}{\kappa_m^2} \quad (m = 1, 2, 3, \dots).$$
*Numerically:*
- $m = 1:\quad \kappa_1 \approx 1.875104068712, \qquad \lambda_1 = +1/\kappa_1^2 \approx \mathbf{+0.284412871855}$;
- $m = 2:\quad \kappa_2 \approx 4.694091132974, \qquad \lambda_2 = -1/\kappa_2^2 \approx \mathbf{-0.045383393443}$;
- $m = 3:\quad \kappa_3 \approx 7.854757438238, \qquad \lambda_3 = +1/\kappa_3^2 \approx \mathbf{+0.016208187185}$.

**Corollaries:**
1. **Asymptotic Flip Probability of an Alternating Saw:** $\lim_{n\to\infty} \mathbb{P}(\text{flip} \mid (\text{`+-`})^n) = \lambda_1 = \kappa_1^{-2} \approx 0.284412871855$.
2. **Exact Per-Turn Memory Compression Factor:** Each sign switch contracts the projectively normalized hidden-height profile toward $\phi_1$ at the asymptotic rate
   $$\rho_{\text{turn}} = \frac{\lambda_1}{|\lambda_2|} = \left(\frac{\kappa_2}{\kappa_1}\right)^2 \approx \mathbf{6.266893025771}.$$
3. **Self-Adjoint Alternating Spectrum & Nested Rayleigh Brackets (`READ-0083`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794694509` & `1794727076`, Jee-AGY `1794726847`):** Because $K(x, t) = (1 - x - t)\mathbf{1}_{x+t\le 1} = K(t, x)$, $T$ is self-adjoint on $L^2([0, 1])$, and setting $u_j := T\phi_j = \lambda_j\phi_j$ ($u_j''(x) = \phi_j(1-x)$, $u_j(1)=u_j'(1)=0$) gives via one integration by parts $\lambda_j = \int_0^1 \phi_j(x)u_j''(1-x)\,dx = -\int_0^1 u_j'(x)u_j'(1-x)\,dx = -\kappa_j^{-2}\phi_j(0)\phi_j(1) = (-1)^{j-1}\kappa_j^{-2}$. Consequently, in $I_0^{(m)} = \langle \mathbf{1}, T^m\mathbf{1}\rangle = \sum_{j\ge 1} c_j^2 \lambda_j^m$ ($c_j = \langle \mathbf{1}, \phi_j\rangle \neq 0$), all even powers $T^{2k}$ are positive-definite while odd powers flip the sign of $j = 2, 4, \dots$, so the Bayesian mass ratios $r_m := I_0^{(m+1)} / I_0^{(m)} = \frac{3}{10}, \frac{71}{252}, \frac{182}{639}, \frac{45\,541}{160\,160}, \frac{168\,388}{592\,033}$ form a strictly nested alternating bracket $[r_{2k}, r_{2k-1}] \ni \lambda_1$ (`0.28174603 < 0.28441287 < 0.28482003`), and finite-step compression ratios oscillate (`8.62 -> 4.46 -> 4.75 -> 4.03`) around $6.267$.
4. **Rayleigh–Cantilever Projection Identity (Jee A `1794304317`, `rayleigh_cantilever_spectral_expansion_2026_11_10.py`):** For the unit-norm clamped-free cantilever eigenfunctions $\hat{\psi}_r$ ($\|\hat{\psi}_r\|_{L^2(0,1)} = 1$, $\hat{\psi}_r(1) = 2$), integrating $f_1(u) = 1 - u$ against $\hat{\psi}_r = \kappa_r^{-4}\hat{\psi}_r^{(4)}$ twice by parts yields the exact projection law and Parseval closure
   $$\mathbf{\langle f_1, \hat{\psi}_r\rangle_{L^2(0,1)} = 2\lambda_r = \frac{2(-1)^{r-1}}{\kappa_r^2}}, \qquad \sum_{r=1}^\infty \langle f_1, \hat{\psi}_r\rangle^2 = 4\sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\kappa_r^4} = \|f_1\|_{L^2(0,1)}^2 = \mathbf{\frac{1}{3}} \quad (97.14\%\text{ in }r=1),$$
   so that for every $k \ge 0$, $T^k f_1 = 2\sum_{r=1}^\infty \lambda_r^{k+1}\hat{\psi}_r$.
5. **Free-End Cantilever Trace Theorem & Unit-Fraction Fredholm Determinant (Den B `1794304520`, `cantilever_trace_zeta_and_triad_audit_den_b.py`; Midpoint Resolvent & Unit-Fraction Fredholm Determinant by Petrovich Antigravity/Argon 11:20, `verify_all_theorems.py`):** Combining $\langle f_1, \hat{\psi}_r\rangle = 2\lambda_r$ with $\hat{\psi}_r(0) = (-1)^{r-1}\hat{\psi}_r(1) = 2(-1)^{r-1}$ and $(T\hat{\psi}_r)(x) = \lambda_r\hat{\psi}_r(x)$ proves that for every integer $m \ge 1$ the operator trace and spectral zeta sum of $T^m$ equal one-fourth of the free-end polynomial value $(T^{m-1} f_1)(0)$ and are **exact rationals**:
   $$\mathbf{\operatorname{Tr}(T^m) = \sum_{r=1}^\infty \lambda_r^m = \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{(-1)^{(r-1)m}}{\kappa_r^{2m}} = \frac{1}{4}\bigl(T^{m-1} f_1\bigr)(0) = \frac{1}{4}\bigl\langle T^a f_1, T^b f_1\bigr\rangle_{L^2(0,1)} \in \mathbb{Q}} \quad (a + b = m - 2\text{ for }m \ge 2).$$
   Moreover, under the midpoint reflection coordinate $y = x - \frac{1}{2} \in [-\frac{1}{2}, \frac{1}{2}]$ ($x \leftrightarrow 1-x \iff y \leftrightarrow -y$), the resolvent BVP $g_z''(x) = z\,g_z(1-x)$ ($g_z = (I - z T)^{-1}f_1$, $g_z(1)=0, g_z'(1)=-1$) decouples into even $\cosh(\sqrt{z}\,y)$ and odd $\sin(\sqrt{z}\,y)$ modes, factoring the cantilever secular function $\frac{1}{2}(\cosh\kappa\cos\kappa + 1) = \det(I - \kappa^4 T^2) = \det(I - \kappa^2 T)\det(I + \kappa^2 T)$ into the **exact unit-fraction Fredholm determinant**:
   $$\mathbf{\Delta(z) := \det(I - z T) = \prod_{r=1}^\infty (1 - z\lambda_r) = \cosh\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2}\cos\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2} - \sinh\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2}\sin\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2} = \sum_{m=0}^\infty \frac{(-1)^{\lfloor(m+1)/2\rfloor}}{2^m (2m)!}\,z^m = 1 - \frac{z}{4} - \frac{z^2}{96} + \frac{z^3}{5760} + \frac{z^4}{645\,120} - \frac{z^5}{116\,121\,600} - \dots}$$
   with free-end resolvent trace generating function $\sum_{m=1}^\infty \operatorname{Tr}(T^m)z^{m-1} = \frac{1}{4}g_z(0) = \frac{\cosh(\sqrt{z}/2)\sin(\sqrt{z}/2)}{4(\sqrt{z}/2)\Delta(z)} = -\frac{\Delta'(z)}{\Delta(z)}$ and Newton–Girard unit-fraction recurrence $\operatorname{Tr}(T^m) = \frac{(-1)^{\lfloor(m-1)/2\rfloor}m}{2^m (2m)!} - \sum_{j=1}^{m-1}\frac{(-1)^{\lfloor(j+1)/2\rfloor}}{2^j (2j)!}\operatorname{Tr}(T^{m-j})$, giving in exact `Fraction` arithmetic (matching 200-root spectral sums to $10^{-18}\text{--}10^{-21}$):
   $$\operatorname{Tr}(T) = \mathbf{\frac{1}{4}}, \quad \operatorname{Tr}(T^2) = \mathbf{\frac{1}{12}}, \quad \operatorname{Tr}(T^3) = \mathbf{\frac{11}{480}}, \quad \operatorname{Tr}(T^4) = \mathbf{\frac{11}{1680}}, \quad \operatorname{Tr}(T^5) = \mathbf{\frac{2701}{1\,451\,520}}, \quad \operatorname{Tr}(T^6) = \mathbf{\frac{2641}{4\,989\,600}}, \quad \dots, \quad \operatorname{Tr}(T^{10}) = \mathbf{\frac{3\,291\,373\,231}{950\,352\,346\,944\,000}}.$$
6. **Cayley–Fredholm Slope EGF, Midpoint Resolvent Unification, Wick-Rotation Trace–Mass Duality & Centrosymmetric Wronskian Conservation Law (Jee A `1794339588`, Den B `1794340355`, Φ-Hausmaster `phi_independent_check_cayley_fredholm_20261110.py`, and Petrovich Antigravity/Carbon 09:20, `verify_all_theorems.py`):** Solving both resolvent BVPs $R_z''(x) = z R_z(1-x)$ ($R_z = (I-zT)^{-1}\mathbf{1}$, $R_z(1)=1, R_z'(1)=0$) and $F_z''(x) = z F_z(1-x)$ ($F_z = (I-zT)^{-1}f_1$, $F_z(1)=0, F_z'(1)=-1$) around the midpoint $y = x - \frac{1}{2}$ yields closed profiles on $[0, 1]$ (**Den B `1794340355`**):
   $$R_z(x) = \frac{\cos\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2}\cosh\bigl(\sqrt{z}(x-\frac{1}{2})\bigr) - \sinh\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2}\sin\bigl(\sqrt{z}(x-\frac{1}{2})\bigr)}{\Delta(z)}, \qquad F_z(x) = \frac{\sin\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2}\cosh\bigl(\sqrt{z}(x-\frac{1}{2})\bigr) - \cosh\frac{\sqrt{z}}{2}\sin\bigl(\sqrt{z}(x-\frac{1}{2})\bigr)}{\sqrt{z}\,\Delta(z)}.$$
   Evaluating at $x = 0$ and $x = \frac{1}{2}$ unifies the alternating walk counts $A_m = (2m)!\,H_0^{(m)}$ ($H_0^{(m)} := (T^m\mathbf{1})(0) = -(T^m f_1)'(0)$), the Bayesian cell masses $I_0^{(m)} := \int_0^1 (T^m\mathbf{1})(x)\,dx$, and the cantilever traces $\Theta_z := \sum_{k=1}^\infty \operatorname{Tr}(T^k)z^{k-1} = -\frac{\Delta'(z)}{\Delta(z)}$:
   - **Cayley–Fredholm EGF for Free-End Slopes $H_0^{(m)}$ and Alternating Walk Counts $A_m$ (Jee A `1794339588` & Den B `1794340355`):**
     $$\mathbf{H_z(0) := \sum_{m=0}^\infty H_0^{(m)} z^m = R_z(0) = -F_z'(0) = \frac{\Delta(-z)}{\Delta(z)} = \frac{\det(I + z T)}{\det(I - z T)}}, \qquad \sum_{m=0}^\infty A_m \frac{w^{2m}}{(2m)!} = \frac{\Delta(-w^2)}{\Delta(w^2)}$$
     ($A_0\dots A_8 = 1, 1, 3, 26, 413, 10\,576, 397\,023, 20\,551\,376, 1\,402\,815\,833$).
   - **Unit-Fraction Bayesian Mass GF & Wick-Rotation Trace–Mass Duality (Jee A `1794339588` & Petrovich Carbon 09:20):** Since $\int_0^1 \Delta(z u^2)\,du = \sum_{m=0}^\infty \frac{(-1)^{\lfloor(m+1)/2\rfloor}}{2^m (2m+1)!}z^m = \frac{\sinh(\sqrt{z}/2)\cos(\sqrt{z}/2)}{\sqrt{z}/2} = -4\,\Delta'(-z)$, the Bayesian mass GF $I_z := \sum_{m=0}^\infty I_0^{(m)} z^m = \langle \mathbf{1}, R_z\rangle$ is the exact Wick-rotated partner of $\Theta_z$ twisted by $H_z(0)$:
     $$\mathbf{I_z = \frac{\int_0^1 \Delta(z u^2)\,du}{\Delta(z)} = -\frac{4\,\Delta'(-z)}{\Delta(z)} = 4\,\Theta_{-z}\,H_z(0) \iff I_0^{(m)} = 4\sum_{k=1}^{m+1} (-1)^{k-1}\operatorname{Tr}(T^k)\,H_0^{(m+1-k)}}.$$
   - **Centrosymmetric Wronskian Conservation Law & Midpoint Pythagorean Identity (Petrovich Carbon 09:20 & Den B `1794340355`):** For the Wronskian $w_z(x) := R_z(x)F_z'(x) - R_z'(x)F_z(x)$, $w_z'(x) = z\bigl(R_z(x)F_z(1-x) - R_z(1-x)F_z(x)\bigr)$ is odd about $x = \frac{1}{2}$, so $w_z(x) = w_z(1-x)$ for all $x \in [0, 1]$. Evaluating $w_z(0) = w_z(1) = -1$ locks the three generating functions $(H_z(0), I_z, \Theta_z)$ into the quadratic conservation law, while $x = \frac{1}{2}$ eliminates the trigonometric numerators of $R_z(\frac{1}{2}) = \frac{\cos(\sqrt{z}/2)}{\Delta(z)}$ and $F_z(\frac{1}{2}) = \frac{\sin(\sqrt{z}/2)}{\sqrt{z}\Delta(z)}$:
     $$\mathbf{H_z(0)^2 - 4z\,I_z\,\Theta_z = 1 \iff \Delta(z)^2 - \Delta(-z)^2 = -16z\,\Delta'(z)\Delta'(-z) = -\sinh(\sqrt{z})\sin(\sqrt{z})},$$
     $$\mathbf{w_z\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\bigr) = -\Delta(z)^{-1}, \qquad R_z\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\bigr)^2 + z\,F_z\bigl(\tfrac{1}{2}\bigr)^2 = \Delta(z)^{-2} = \det(I - z T)^{-2}}.$$
   - **Self-Similar Wronskian Scaling Law on $[0, 1]$ & Simplex Proof of $I_z$ (Jee A `1794389764`, verified by Φ-Hausmaster `phi_independent_check_wronskian_scaling_20261111.py` & Jee-AGY `READ-0067`):** Evaluating $w_z(x)$ at arbitrary $x \in [0, 1]$ shows that the Wronskian is a compressed copy of $\Delta(z)$ around the midpoint $x = \frac{1}{2}$, uniting $x \in \{0, \frac{1}{2}, 1\}$ and giving a three-line simplex-antisymmetry proof of $I_z = \int_0^1 (-w_z(x))\,dx$:
     $$\mathbf{-w_z(x) = R_z'(x)F_z(x) - R_z(x)F_z'(x) = \frac{\Delta\!\bigl(z(2x - 1)^2\bigr)}{\Delta(z)}} \quad (\forall x \in [0, 1]), \qquad \sum_{j=0}^m \Delta_j\,W_{m-j}(x) = \Delta_m\,(2x - 1)^{2m}.$$
7. **Gohberg–Semencul 2D Kernel Reconstruction, Centrosymmetric Bezoutian Identity, and Universal $1/4$ Resolvent Barycenter Law (Den B `1794390265` & `1794645335`, Φ-Hausmaster `phi_independent_check_gohberg_semencul_bezoutian_20261111.py`, Jee-AGY `READ-0067`, and Petrovich `2026-11-14`, `verify_all_theorems.py`):**
   - **Rank-1 Anticommutator & Gohberg–Semencul 2D Kernel Reconstruction (Den B):** For $D = \frac{d}{dx}$, $(DT + TD)h = -h(0)f_1$ ($D T + T D = -f_1 \otimes \delta_0$), so $K_m(x, y)$ satisfies $(\partial_x + (-1)^m\partial_y)K_m(x, y) = -\sum_{k=0}^{m-2}(-1)^k(T^k f_1)(x)(T^{m-2-k}f_1)(y)$ with $K_m(1,\cdot)=K_m(\cdot,1)=0$, reconstructing the entire 2D kernel $K_m(x, y)$ on $[0, 1]^2$ from the single vector $f_1(x) = 1 - x$ ($K_{2n}(x, y) = \sum_{k=0}^{2n-2}(-1)^k\int_0^{1-\max(x,y)}(T^k f_1)(x+u)(T^{2n-2-k}f_1)(y+u)\,du$ and $K_{2n+1}(x, y) = \mathbf{1}_{x+y\le 1}(T^{2n}f_1)(x+y) + \sum_{k=0}^{2n-1}(-1)^k\int_0^{\min(y,1-x)}(T^k f_1)(x+u)(T^{2n-1-k}f_1)(y-u)\,du$) and closing the purely analytic proof of $\operatorname{Tr}(T^m) = \frac{1}{4}(T^{m-1}f_1)(0)$.
   - **Rank-2 Wave Commutator & Centrosymmetric Bezoutian Reflection Identity (Den B):** Since $[D^2, T] = \mathbf{1}\otimes\delta_0 + f_1\otimes\delta_0'$, the 2D resolvent kernel $G_z(x, y) = \sum_{m\ge 1} z^{m-1}K_m(x, y)$ cancels all piecewise kinks under $(x, y) \mapsto (1-x, 1-y)$, leaving an exact rank-2 Bezoutian polynomial whose antidiagonal normal derivative is Jee A's self-similar Wronskian:
     $$\mathbf{G_z(x, y) - G_z(1-x, 1-y) = R_z(1-x)F_z(y) - F_z(1-x)R_z(y)}, \qquad \mathbf{-\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\Bigl[G_z(x, y) - G_z(1-x, 1-y)\Bigr]_{y=1-x} = -w_z(x) = \frac{\Delta\!\bigl(z(2x-1)^2\bigr)}{\Delta(z)}}.$$
   - **Christoffel–Darboux Diagonal Law & Universal $1/4$ Barycenter Invariant (Petrovich `2026-11-14`):** Summing $K_{2n}(x, x)$ gives the exact diagonal Green's function of $T^2$: $G^{(2)}_{z^2}(x, x) := \frac{G_z(x,x)-G_{-z}(x,x)}{2z} = \int_x^1 F_z(s)F_{-z}(s)\,ds$. Evaluating at $x = 0$ ($\int_0^1 F_z(x)F_{-z}(x)\,dx = \frac{F_z(0)-F_{-z}(0)}{2z} = 4\operatorname{Tr}(T^2(I-z^2 T^2)^{-1})$) and integrating over $x \in [0, 1]$ ($\int_0^1 x\,F_z(x)F_{-z}(x)\,dx = \frac{F_z(0)-F_{-z}(0)}{8z} = \operatorname{Tr}(T^2(I-z^2 T^2)^{-1})$) proves that the center of mass of the Wick-conjugate resolvent density $F_z(x)F_{-z}(x)$ on $[0, 1]$ is strictly $z$-invariant:
     $$\mathbf{-\frac{d}{dx}\,G^{(2)}_{z^2}(x, x) = F_z(x)\,F_{-z}(x), \qquad \frac{\int_0^1 x\,F_z(x)\,F_{-z}(x)\,dx}{\int_0^1 F_z(x)\,F_{-z}(x)\,dx} \equiv \frac{1}{4} \qquad (\forall z \in \mathbb{C})}.$$
8. **First-Order Wick–Darboux ODE System, Fourth-Order Bifurcated Beam Equations, Pointwise Density $\rho_{z^2}(x)$, Finite-$z$ Quadratic Antiderivative, Exact Derivative Law $\hat{\rho}_r(x) \equiv -\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{dx}[\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2]$, Semiclassical $\operatorname{Unif}([0,1])$ vs $\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)$ Duality, and Closed-Form Bilateral MGF, Variance, Skewness, Kurtosis & Universal Central Moment Syzygy (Den B `1794650254` & `1794685640` `rev 100`, Jee A `1794650617` & `1794685596` `rev 100`, Jee-AGY `1794651347` `READ-0071` & `READ-0077`–`READ-0081`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794646944`, `1794673143`, `1794676320`, `1794680033` & `1794683683`/`5257`/`5895`/`6202`, Librarian & Ariadne `1794679393`, and Petrovich `2026-11-14` `1794684872`, `verify_all_theorems.py`):**
   - **First-Order Wick–Darboux ODE System, Exact Derivative Factorization, and Integral-Free Finite-$z$ Quadratic Antiderivative of $G^{(2)}_{z^2}(x, x)$ (Den B `1794650254` & Jee A `1794650617`, `1794685596` `rev 100`):** Because $(D T + T D)h = -h(0)f_1$ intertwines $(I - zT)$ and $(I + zT)$ on $C^1([0, 1])$, the 2nd-order nonlocal resolvent BVPs collapse to a local 1st-order Wick–Darboux system on $[0, 1]$:
     $$\mathbf{R_z'(x) = -z\,H_z(0)\,F_{-z}(x), \qquad F_z'(x) = -R_{-z}(x) - z\,F_z(0)\,F_{-z}(x), \qquad R_z'(x)\,R_{-z}'(x) = -z^2\,F_z(x)\,F_{-z}(x) = -z^2\,\rho_{z^2}(x)},$$
     giving an integration-by-parts proof of the Universal $1/4$ Barycenter Law via $\bigl(R_z(x)F_{-z}(x) + R_{-z}(x)F_z(x)\bigr)' = -2\rho_{z^2}(x) - \frac{F_z(0)-F_{-z}(0)}{z}\bigl(R_z'(x) + R_{-z}'(x)\bigr)$. Furthermore, differentiating the quadratic form $R_z(x)^2 - z H_z(0) F_{-z}(x)^2$ and using the centrosymmetric Wronskian conservation law $H_z(0)^2 - 1 = 4z\mathcal{I}_0(z)\Theta_z = 2z^2\mathcal{I}_0(z)F_z(0)$ gives an exact quadratic potential for $\rho_{z^2}(x)$ at **every finite $z \in \mathbb{C}$** (**Jee A `1794685596` `rev 100`**):
     $$\mathbf{\frac{d}{dx}\Bigl[R_z(x)^2 - z\,H_z(0)\,F_{-z}(x)^2\Bigr] = -2z^2\,\mathcal{I}_0(z)\,\rho_{z^2}(x), \qquad 2z^2\,\mathcal{I}_0(z)\,G^{(2)}_{z^2}(x, x) \equiv R_z(x)^2 - z\,H_z(0)\,F_{-z}(x)^2 - 1 \qquad (\forall x \in [0, 1])},$$
     and the **Finite-$z$ Moment Shift Law** $\mathbf{2z^2\,\mathcal{I}_0(z)\,m_p(z^2) \equiv p\int_0^1 x^{p-1}\bigl[R_z(x)^2 - z\,H_z(0)\,F_{-z}(x)^2 - 1\bigr]dx}$ ($\forall p \ge 1$).
   - **Fourth-Order Bifurcated Euler–Bernoulli Beam & Exact Pointwise Closed Form of $\rho_{z^2}(x)$ (Jee A `1794650617`, Den B `1794650254`, and Petrovich `2026-11-14`):** Splitting $\rho_{z^2}(x) = F_z(x)F_{-z}(x)$ into its midpoint-symmetric and midpoint-antisymmetric parts $\rho_\pm(x) := \rho_{z^2}(x) \pm \rho_{z^2}(1-x)$ ($u := 2x - 1 \in [-1, 1]$, $k := \sqrt{z}$) bifurcates into two decoupled 4th-order beam equations of opposite spectral sign (**Jee A**):
     $$\mathbf{D_x^4\,\rho_+(x) = -4z^2\,\rho_+(x)} \quad (\text{Kelvin }e^{(\pm 1\pm i)kx/2}\text{ modes}), \qquad \mathbf{D_x^4\,\rho_-(x) = +16z^2\,\rho_-(x)} \quad (\text{Rayleigh }\sin ku, \sinh ku\text{ modes}),$$
     yielding the exact pointwise closed form on $[0, 1]$ (correcting the half-angle typo in the odd hyperbolic branch of `wick_darboux_ode_and_barycenter_variance_den_b.py` docstring L48–49 to the true $\ker(D_x^4 - 16z^2)$ mode, **Petrovich `2026-11-14`**):
     $$\mathbf{\rho_{z^2}(x) = \frac{\sinh(kx)\sin(kx) + \sinh\bigl(k(1-x)\bigr)\sin\bigl(k(1-x)\bigr) - \frac{1}{2}\Bigl[\sinh(k)\sin\bigl(k(2x-1)\bigr) + \sin(k)\sinh\bigl(k(2x-1)\bigr)\Bigr]}{k^2\,(1 + \cosh k\cos k)}}.$$
   - **Exact All-Mode Wick–Darboux Derivative Law $\hat{\rho}_r(x) \equiv -\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{dx}[\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2]$, Parity Split (`tanh` vs `coth`), Pure-Beam Variance & Kurtosis, and Semiclassical $\operatorname{Unif}([0,1])$ vs $\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)$ Duality (Jee-AGY `READ-0078`–`READ-0082` `1794679811`, Petrovich `1794684872`, Den B `1794650254` `rev 99` & `1794685640` `rev 100`, Jee A `1794685596` `rev 100`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794673143`–`1794686202` `phi_independent_check_wick_derivative_law_20261114.py` & `phi_check_two_continuum_proofs_20261114.py`, with pure-$\phi_1^2$ identification of `0.1935 / 4.0` noted by Librarian & Ariadne `1794679393` and attribution audited by Librarian & Aglaya `1794693760`):** Because $1 + \cosh\kappa_r\cos\kappa_r = 2\Delta(\kappa_r^2)\Delta(-\kappa_r^2) = 0$ splits by mode parity into **odd modes $r = 1, 3, \dots$** ($\Delta(+\kappa_r^2) = 0$, $\lambda_r = +\kappa_r^{-2}$, $t_r := \frac{\sinh\kappa_r-\sin\kappa_r}{\cosh\kappa_r+\cos\kappa_r} = \tanh\frac{\kappa_r}{2} = \cot\frac{\kappa_r}{2}$) and **even modes $r = 2, 4, \dots$** ($\Delta(-\kappa_r^2) = 0$, $\lambda_r = -\kappa_r^{-2}$, $t_r = \coth\frac{\kappa_r}{2} = -\tan\frac{\kappa_r}{2}$), dividing **Den B's (`rev 99`, `bus:1794650254_180768_6ae016`, `agy_1794652589_4df6b0`)** 1st-order ODE $R_z'(x) = -z R_z(0) F_{-z}(x)$ by $R_z(0)$ at $z \to \lambda_r^{-1} = (-1)^{r-1}\kappa_r^2$ (**Jee-AGY `READ-0079` `1794679811`**) — or directly on the continuum from the midpoint resolvents or 1-step integration by parts $(DT+TD)h = -h(0)f_1$ (`READ-0080`) — proves that $\mathbf{F_{-\lambda_r^{-1}}(x) \equiv -\frac{\lambda_r}{2}\,\hat{\psi}_r'(x)}$ for **all** $r \ge 1$. Consequently, every normalized pole Wick–Darboux density $\hat{\rho}_r(x)$ ($\hat{\rho}_\infty \equiv \hat{\rho}_1$) is **not** the pure squared mode $\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2 = \phi_r(1-x)^2$ ($\hat{\psi}_r(0)^2 = \phi_r(1)^2 = 4$), but minus one-fourth its spatial derivative:
     $$\mathbf{\hat{\rho}_r(x) \equiv -\frac{1}{4}\frac{d}{dx}\Bigl[\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\Bigr] = -\frac{1}{2}\,\hat{\psi}_r(x)\,\hat{\psi}_r'(x) = \frac{1}{2}\,\phi_r(1-x)\,\phi_r'(1-x) \qquad \forall r \ge 1,\; \forall x \in [0, 1]}.$$
     Integrating by parts on $[0, 1]$ and evaluating at $x = 0$ immediately yields:
     1. **Free-End Slope Resonance ($\Phi$ `1794676320` & `READ-0079`):** Since $\phi_r(1) = \hat{\psi}_r(0) = 2$, the factor $\frac{1}{2}$ cancels $\phi_r(1) = 2$ at the free end: $\mathbf{\hat{\rho}_r(0) = \frac{1}{2}\phi_r(1)\phi_r'(1) \equiv \phi_r'(1) = -\hat{\psi}_r'(0) = 2\kappa_r t_r}$ (at $r = 1$: $\mathbf{2\kappa_1\tanh(\kappa_1/2) = 2.7530109693450695559\ldots}$);
     2. **Universal Moment Shift Law, Pure-Beam Variance & Kurtosis (`READ-0078`–`READ-0081`, Petrovich `1794684872`, Den B `1794685640` `rev 100`, Φ `1794673143` & `1794685257`/`6202`):** For every $p \ge 1$ and $r \ge 1$, $\mathbf{\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\rho}_r}[x^p] \equiv \frac{p}{4}\,\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\psi}_r^2}[x^{p-1}] = \frac{p}{4}\,\mathbb{E}_{\phi_r^2}[(1-u)^{p-1}]}$ and $\mathbf{\int_0^1 e^{s(2x-1)}\,\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \equiv \frac{2\bigl(\mathcal{M}_{\hat{\rho}_r}(s) - e^{-s}\bigr)}{s}}$. This gives for every mode $r \ge 1$ the exact mean, variance, skewness, and kurtosis of the **pure squared cantilever beam mode** $\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2$ ($\phi_r(u)^2$):
        $$\mathbf{\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\psi}_r^2}[x] = \frac{1}{8} + 2\sigma^2(\hat{\rho}_r) = \frac{1}{2} - \frac{2t_r^2}{\kappa_r^2}} \quad \bigl(\text{at }r=1:\; \mathbf{0.193461954986991607\ldots},\; \mathbb{E}_{\phi_1^2}[u] = \mathbf{0.806538045013008393\ldots}\bigr),$$
        $$\mathbf{\sigma^2(\hat{\psi}_r^2) = \frac{1}{12} - \frac{t_r^2}{2\kappa_r^2} + \frac{t_r}{\kappa_r^3} - \frac{4t_r^4}{\kappa_r^4}} \quad \bigl(\text{at }r=1:\; \mathbf{0.02407971602667635170\ldots},\text{ closing }\Phi\text{'s }0.0241!\bigr),$$
        $$\mathbf{\mu_3^{\text{cent}}(\hat{\psi}_r^2) = -\frac{t_r^2}{\kappa_r^2} + \frac{6t_r}{\kappa_r^3} - \frac{6 + 3t_r^4}{\kappa_r^4} + \frac{6t_r^3}{\kappa_r^5} - \frac{16t_r^6}{\kappa_r^6}} \quad \bigl(\text{at }r=1:\; \mathbf{+0.00377924838568549131\ldots},\; \gamma_1(\hat{\psi}_1^2) = \mathbf{+1.0114126817544383\ldots}\bigr),$$
        $$\mathbf{\mu_4^{\text{cent}}(\hat{\psi}_r^2) = \frac{1}{80} - \frac{t_r^2}{4\kappa_r^2} + \frac{3t_r}{2\kappa_r^3} - \frac{3 + 20t_r^4}{2\kappa_r^4} + \frac{48t_r^3}{\kappa_r^5} - \frac{48t_r^2 + 12t_r^6}{\kappa_r^6} + \frac{24t_r^5}{\kappa_r^7} - \frac{48t_r^8}{\kappa_r^8}}$$
        (at $r=1$: $\mu_4^{\text{cent}}(\hat{\psi}_1^2) = \mathbf{0.00209484117836357533\ldots}$ and $\mathbf{\kappa_4(\hat{\psi}_1^2) = 3.612837102711000335\ldots > 3}$, so $r = 1$ is the **unique** right-skewed and leptokurtic pure cantilever mode, whereas all $r \ge 2$ have $\gamma_1(\hat{\psi}_r^2) < 0$ and $\kappa_4(\hat{\psi}_r^2) < 3$, achieving their **global minimum across all $r \ge 1$ at $r = 3$**, $\mathbf{\kappa_4(\hat{\psi}_3^2) = 1.7008578572295902\ldots < \kappa_4(\hat{\psi}_2^2) = 1.7145824618898587\ldots < \frac{9}{5} = 1.8}$, and then increasing monotonically from below toward $\kappa_4 \to \frac{9}{5} = 1.8$ of $\operatorname{Unif}([0, 1])$ with asymptotic deficit $\kappa_4(\hat{\psi}_r^2) - \frac{9}{5} = -\frac{24}{5\kappa_r^2} + \frac{72}{5\kappa_r^3} + O(\kappa_r^{-4})$, **Den B `1794736594` `rev 101` & Φ `1794737048`**!);
     3. **Semiclassical Duality ($r \to \infty$), 2-Line Symbolic MGF Proof, Strict Monotonicity from Below of $\mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\hat{\rho}_r)$, and Finite-$n$ Origin of $\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)$ Constants (Petrovich `1794684872` & `1794732060`, Den B `1794685640` `rev 100`, Φ `1794732301`, `READ-0085`–`READ-0086`):** As $r \to \infty$ ($\kappa_r \sim (r - \frac{1}{2})\pi \to \infty$, $t_r \to 1$), the pure squared beam modes equidistribute on $[0, 1]$ ($\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \rightharpoonup \operatorname{Unif}([0, 1])$, $\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\psi}_r^2}[x^{p-1}] \to \frac{1}{p}$), whereas because the free-end amplitude is pinned at $\hat{\psi}_r(0)^2 \equiv 4$, integration by parts $\int_0^1 g(x)\hat{\rho}_r(x)\,dx = g(0) + \frac{1}{4}\int_0^1 g'(x)\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \to \frac{3}{4}g(0) + \frac{1}{4}g(1)$ — or **directly in 2 lines from the closed-form bilateral MGF** $\lim_{r\to\infty}\mathcal{M}_{\hat{\rho}_r}(s) = \cosh(s) - \frac{1}{2}\sinh(s) = \frac{3}{4}e^{-s} + \frac{1}{4}e^s = \mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)}(s)$ and $\lim_{r\to\infty}\mathcal{M}_{\hat{\psi}_r^2}(s) = \frac{2(\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)}(s) - e^{-s})}{s} = \frac{\sinh s}{s} = \mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Unif}([0,1])}(s)$ (**Petrovich `1794732060` & Φ `1794732301`**) — proves that $\hat{\rho}_r(x) = -\frac{1}{4}(\hat{\psi}_r^2)'$ converges weakly to the **two-atom boundary measure $\frac{3}{4}\delta_0 + \frac{1}{4}\delta_1 = \operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)$**! Moreover, expanding $\mathcal{M}_{\hat{\rho}_r}(s)$ to order $O(\kappa_r^{-2})$ shows that for **every** $p \ge 2$, $\mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\hat{\rho}_r) = \mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)) - c_p\frac{t_r^2}{\kappa_r^2} + O(\kappa_r^{-3})$ with strictly positive coefficient $\mathbf{c_p = \frac{p(p-1)}{8}\bigl[(\frac{3}{4})^{p-2} + 3(-\frac{1}{4})^{p-2}\bigr] = 1, \frac{9}{8}, \frac{9}{4}, \frac{225}{64}, \frac{675}{128}, \frac{3969}{512}, \dots > 0}$ (since $(\frac{3}{4})^m + 3(-\frac{1}{4})^m \ge 0$ for all $m \ge 0$), and because $\kappa_r/\tanh(\kappa_r/2)$ is strictly increasing for $r \ge 1$, **every central moment $\mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\hat{\rho}_r)$ ($p \ge 2$) is strictly increasing in $r \ge 1$ and approaches $\mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4))$ monotonically from below** (`symbolic_bernoulli_mgf_and_monotone_law_2026_11_15.py`). Remarkably, the central moments $\mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)) = \frac{3(-1)^p + 3^p}{4^{p+1}} = \mathbf{0, \frac{3}{16}, \frac{3}{32}, \frac{21}{256}, \frac{15}{256}, \frac{183}{4096}, \dots}$ appear already at **every finite $n \ge 0$** as the exact leading coefficient of $m_0^{(2n)}$ in the finite-$n$ staircase $m_p^{(2n)} = \int_0^1 x^p\rho^{(2n)}(x)\,dx$ (**Den B `1794685640` `rev 100`**).
   - **Closed-Form Bilateral Moment-Generating Function $\mathcal{M}(s; z) := \mathbb{E}_z[e^{s(2x-1)}]$, 4-Step Moment Recurrence, Variance $\sigma^2(z)$, Skewness $\mu_3^{\text{cent}}(z)$, Kurtosis $\mu_4^{\text{cent}}(z)$, Universal Central Moment Syzygy, Closed-Form Staircase Coefficients $(\alpha_k, \beta_k, \gamma_k)$ & $(A_k, B_k, \Gamma_k)$, All-Order Syzygy Polynomials $Q_K(y)$, and Pure-Beam Duality $B_K(y)$ (Jee-AGY `READ-0071`, `READ-0077`, `READ-0085`–`READ-0086`, Den B `1794650254`, `1794685640` `rev 100` & `1794736594` `rev 101`, Jee A `1794650617`, `1794685596` `rev 100` & `1794736035` `rev 101`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794673143`, `1794685895`, `1794736341`, `1794737048` & `1794739445`, and Petrovich `2026-11-14/15` `1794739213`):** Setting $S_\pm(k) := \cosh k\sin k \pm \sinh k\cos k$, $S_\times(k) := \sinh k\sin k$, and $D(k) := 1 + \cosh k\cos k = 2\Delta(z)\Delta(-z)$ (so $m_0(z^2) = \frac{S_-(k)}{k^3 D(k)}$), integrating $\rho_\pm$ against $e^{s(2x-1)}$ cancels $D(k)$ in the normalization and gives the exact bilateral MGF for all $(s, z) \in \mathbb{C}^2$ (**Petrovich `2026-11-14`**):
     $$\mathbf{\mathcal{M}(s; z) = \frac{k\Bigl[s^3\sinh(s)\,S_\times(k) - \frac{k}{2}s^2\cosh(s)\,S_+(k) + \frac{k^2}{2}s\sinh(s)\,D(k) + \frac{k^3}{4}\cosh(s)\,S_-(k)\Bigr]}{S_-(k)\,\bigl(s^4 + \tfrac{z^2}{4}\bigr)} - \frac{k\Bigl[s^3\cosh(s)\,S_\times(k) - \frac{k}{2}s^2\sinh(s)\,S_+(k) - \frac{k^3}{2}\sinh(s)\,S_-(k)\Bigr]}{S_-(k)\,\bigl(s^4 - z^2\bigr)}},$$
     and at any cantilever pole $z \to \lambda_r^{-1}$ ($D(\kappa_r) = 0$, $S_\times(\kappa_r)/S_-(\kappa_r) = t_r$, $S_+(\kappa_r)/S_-(\kappa_r) = t_r^2$):
     $$\mathbf{\mathcal{M}_{\hat{\rho}_r}(s) = \int_0^1 e^{s(2x-1)}\,\hat{\rho}_r(x)\,dx = \frac{\kappa_r\bigl(t_r s^3\sinh s - \frac{\kappa_r t_r^2}{2}s^2\cosh s + \frac{\kappa_r^3}{4}\cosh s\bigr)}{s^4 + \frac{\kappa_r^4}{4}} - \frac{\kappa_r\bigl(t_r s^3\cosh s - \frac{\kappa_r t_r^2}{2}s^2\sinh s - \frac{\kappa_r^3}{2}\sinh s\bigr)}{s^4 - \kappa_r^4}}.$$
     Because the even and odd parts of $\mathcal{M}(s; z)$ have two-term denominators $s^4 + z^2/4$ and $s^4 - z^2$, all midpoint moments $U_p(z) := \mathbb{E}_z[(2x-1)^p]$ obey an exact 4-step algebraic recurrence $p \mapsto p + 4$ (`closed_form_kurtosis_and_4step_recurrence_2026_11_14.py`), recovering $[s^1]\mathcal{M}(s; z) \equiv -\frac{1}{2}$ ($\mathbb{E}_z[x] \equiv \frac{1}{4}$), **Jee-AGY, Den B & Jee A's** exact variance $\sigma^2(z) := \mathbb{E}_z[(x - \frac{1}{4})^2]$ (`READ-0071`), **Petrovich's** exact 3rd central moment $\mu_3^{\text{cent}}(z) := \mathbb{E}_z[(x - \frac{1}{4})^3]$, and **Jee-AGY & Φ-Hausmaster's** exact 4th central moment $\mu_4^{\text{cent}}(z) := \mathbb{E}_z[(x - \frac{1}{4})^4]$ (`READ-0077`–`READ-0078`) together with their dominant-eigenmode limits:
     $$\mathbf{\sigma^2(z) = \frac{3}{16} - \frac{S_+(k) - k\,D(k)}{k^2\,S_-(k)} = \frac{3}{80} - \frac{19}{75\,600}z^2 - \frac{761}{756\,756\,000}z^4 - \frac{81\,931}{19\,451\,656\,224\,000}z^6 - \dots, \qquad \sigma_\infty^2 = \frac{3}{16} - \frac{\tanh^2(\kappa_1/2)}{\kappa_1^2} = 0.034230977493495803\ldots},$$
     $$\mathbf{\mu_3^{\text{cent}}(z) = \frac{3}{32} - \frac{9\bigl(S_+(k) - k\,D(k)\bigr)}{8\,k^2\,S_-(k)} + \frac{3\bigl(2S_\times(k) - k^2\,D(k)\bigr)}{8\,k^3\,S_-(k)} = \frac{1}{160} - \frac{11}{100\,800}z^2 - \frac{1291}{3\,027\,024\,000}z^4 - \dots, \qquad \mu_{3,\infty}^{\text{cent}} = \frac{3}{32} - \frac{9\tanh^2(\kappa_1/2)}{8\kappa_1^2} + \frac{3\tanh(\kappa_1/2)}{4\kappa_1^3} = 0.004832199920426986\ldots},$$
     $$\mathbf{\mu_4^{\text{cent}}(z) = \frac{21}{256} - \frac{6}{z^2} + \frac{27\,S_\times(k)}{4\,k^3\,S_-(k)} - \frac{9\,S_+(k)}{4\,k^2\,S_-(k)} + \frac{7\,D(k)}{8\,k\,S_-(k)} = \frac{39}{8\,960} - \frac{223}{3\,104\,640}z^2 - \frac{31}{113\,008\,896}z^4 - \dots, \qquad \mu_{4,\infty}^{\text{cent}} = \frac{21}{256} - \frac{6}{\kappa_1^4} + \frac{27\tanh(\kappa_1/2)}{4\kappa_1^3} - \frac{9\tanh^2(\kappa_1/2)}{4\kappa_1^2} = 0.003420511461863833\ldots}$$
     (so the dominant Wick–Darboux pole density $\hat{\rho}_\infty(x)$ has exact skewness $\gamma_{1,\infty} = \mu_{3,\infty}^{\text{cent}} / \sigma_\infty^3 = \mathbf{+0.7629848989123056\ldots}$, kurtosis $\kappa_{4,\infty} = \mu_{4,\infty}^{\text{cent}} / \sigma_\infty^4 = \mathbf{2.9191233587409939\ldots}$, and excess kurtosis $\gamma_{2,\infty} = \kappa_{4,\infty} - 3 = \mathbf{-0.0808766412590061\ldots}$, and by **Jee A `1794685596` `rev 100`** the pole syzygy $\mu_{4,\infty}^{\text{cent}} = \frac{183}{256} - \frac{63}{8}\sigma_\infty^2 + 9\mu_{3,\infty}^{\text{cent}} - \frac{6}{\kappa_1^4}$ lifts to the **Universal Central Moment Syzygy** $\mathbf{\mu_{4,\text{cent}}^{(2n)} \equiv \frac{183}{256} - \frac{63}{8}\,\sigma_{2n}^2 + 9\,\mu_{3,\text{cent}}^{(2n)} - 6\,\frac{m_0^{(2n+2)}}{m_0^{(2n)}}}$ at every finite $n \ge 0$!).
   - **Closed-Form Coefficients of the Entire Finite-$z$ & Finite-$n$ Moment Staircase, All-Order Universal Midpoint Syzygy Polynomials $Q_K(2x-1)$ ($p = 4K + 1$, $\forall K \ge 0$), Closed-Form Central Coefficients $\tilde{c}_{K,r}$, Pure-Beam Polynomial Duality $B_K(2x-1)$ ($\forall K \ge 1$), and Cloud Jee's Boundary-Pinned Antiderivative Bridge $P_{4K+1}(x) \leftrightarrow Q_{4K}(x)$ (Jee A `1794736035` `rev 101`, Den B `1794736594` `rev 101`, Petrovich `1794739213`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794736341`, `1794737048` & `1794739445`, Cloud Jee `FROM_CLOUD_JEE_TO_HOUSE_2026-11-15` §1.2, and Jee-AGY `READ-0086`–`READ-0087`):** Clearing $D(k)$ in $\mathcal{M}(s; z)$ yields the linear PDE $\bigl(\partial_s^4 - \frac{3}{4}z^2\partial_s^2 - \frac{1}{4}z^4\bigr)\mathcal{M}_{\text{raw}}(s; z) = \mathcal{R}_{\text{src}}(s; z)$, proving that for **all** $p \ge 0$ and $n \ge 0$, both the raw moments $m_p^{(2n)} = \int_0^1 x^p\rho^{(2n)}(x)\,dx$ and central moments $\mu_p^{\text{cent},(2n)} = \int_0^1 (x - \frac{1}{4})^p\rho^{(2n)}(x)\,dx$ are finite linear combinations of $(m_0^{(2n+2k)}, S_+^{(2n+2k+1)}, S_\times^{(2n+2k+1)})$ with exact closed-form rational coefficients (**Jee A `1794736035` & Den B `1794736594` `rev 101`**):
     $$\mathbf{m_p^{(2n)} = \sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor p/2\rfloor}\left[\alpha_k(p)\,m_0^{(2n+2k)} + \beta_k(p)\,S_+^{(2n+2k+1)} + \gamma_k(p)\,S_\times^{(2n+2k+1)}\right], \qquad \mu_p^{\text{cent},(2n)} = \sum_{k=0}^{\lfloor p/2\rfloor}\left[A_k(p)\,m_0^{(2n+2k)} + B_k(p)\,S_+^{(2n+2k+1)} + \Gamma_k(p)\,S_\times^{(2n+2k+1)}\right]},$$
     where $\alpha_{2j}(p) = (-1)^j\frac{p!}{(p-4j)!\,4^{j+1}}$, $\alpha_{2j+1}(p) = (-1)^{j+1}\frac{p!}{(p-4j-2)!}$, $\beta_{2j}(p) = (-1)^{j+1}\frac{p!\,(1 - 4^{-(2j+1)})}{(p-4j-2)!}$, $\beta_{2j+1}(p) = \gamma_{2j}(p) = 0$, $\gamma_{2j+1}(p) = (-1)^j\frac{2\cdot p!\,(1 + 4^{-(2j+1)})}{(p-4j-3)!}$, and under the binomial shift $u = x - \frac{1}{4}$: $A_0(p) = \mu_p^{\text{cent}}(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4))$, $A_{2j}(p) = (-1)^j\frac{p!\,(3^{p-4j}+3(-1)^p)}{(p-4j)!\,4^{p-3j+1}}$, $A_{2j+1}(p) = (-1)^{j+1}\frac{p!\,(3^{p-4j-2}-(-1)^p)}{(p-4j-2)!\,4^{p-4j-2}}$, $B_{2j}(p) = (-1)^{j+1}\frac{p!\,(1-4^{-(2j+1)})(3^{p-4j-2}-(-1)^p)}{(p-4j-2)!\,4^{p-4j-2}}$, $B_{2j+1}(p)=\Gamma_{2j}(p)=0$, $\Gamma_{2j+1}(p) = (-1)^j\frac{2\cdot p!\,(1+4^{-(2j+1)})(3^{p-4j-3}+(-1)^p)}{(p-4j-3)!\,4^{p-4j-3}}$. Because $(1 \pm i)^4 = -4$ intertwines the odd Rayleigh operator $(D_y^4 - z^2)$ and the even Kelvin operator $(D_y^4 + z^2/4)$ in the midpoint coordinate $y := 2x - 1 \in [-1, 1]$, at every degree $p = 4K + 1$ ($K \ge 0$) all $2K$ boundary terms $S_+^{(2n+4j+1)}, S_\times^{(2n+4j+3)}$ collapse simultaneously into the **closed-form Master Midpoint Syzygy Polynomial Family** $Q_K(y)$ (which contains no lower odd powers $y^1 = y^3 = \dots = y^{4K-1} = 0$, **Petrovich `1794739213` & Φ `1794739445`**):
     $$\mathbf{Q_K(y) = y^{4K+1} + \frac{(-1)^K}{2\cdot 4^K}\,\operatorname{Im}\Bigl[\bigl(y + i\bigr)^{4K+1}\Bigr] = y^{4K+1} + \frac{(-1)^K}{2\cdot 4^K}\sum_{m=0}^{2K}(-1)^m\binom{4K+1}{2m+1}y^{4K-2m} \qquad (y := 2x - 1)},$$
     $$\mathbf{\int_0^1 Q_K(2x - 1)\,\rho^{(2n)}(x)\,dx \equiv 0 \quad (\forall n \ge 0), \qquad \mathbb{E}_z\Bigl[Q_K(2x - 1)\Bigr] \equiv 0 \quad (\forall z \in \mathbb{C}), \qquad \mathbb{E}_{\hat{\rho}_r}\Bigl[Q_K(2x - 1)\Bigr] \equiv 0 \quad (\forall r \ge 1)}.$$
     Substituting $y = 2u - \frac{1}{2}$ ($u := x - \frac{1}{4}$) into $2^{-(4K+1)}Q_K(2u - \frac{1}{2}) = u^{4K+1} + \sum_{r=0}^{4K}\tilde{c}_{K,r}u^r$ gives the explicit closed-form central-moment coefficients $\mathbf{\tilde{c}_{K,r} = \binom{4K+1}{r}\frac{(-1)^{r-1} + \frac{(-1)^K}{2\cdot 4^K}\operatorname{Im}[(-1+2i)^{4K+1-r}]}{4^{4K+1-r}}}$ ($0 \le r \le 4K$), and $O(K^2)$ triangular back-substitution over the $K-1$ lower syzygy slots $\{4j+1 : 1 \le j < K\}$ yields the reduced parameter-free central-moment syzygies for all $K \ge 1$ (recovering **Jee A & Den B's `rev 101`** $p = 5$ syzygy $\mu_5^{\text{cent}} = \frac{5}{4}\mu_4^{\text{cent}} + \frac{5}{8}\mu_3^{\text{cent}} - \frac{15}{64}\sigma^2 - \frac{51}{1024}$ at $K=1$, **Den B's `rev 101`** $p = 9$ syzygy at $K=2$, and $p = 13, 17, 21, \dots$). Furthermore, since $Q_K(-1) \equiv -\frac{1}{2}$ and $Q_K(+1) \equiv +\frac{3}{2}$ for all $K \ge 0$, integrating any polynomial $P(x)$ against $\hat{\rho}_r(x) = -\frac{1}{4}(\hat{\psi}_r^2)'$ with $\hat{\psi}_r(1)^2 = 0, \hat{\psi}_r(0)^2 = 4$ gives **Cloud Jee's Finite-$r$ Integration-by-Parts Identity (`FROM_CLOUD_JEE_TO_HOUSE_2026-11-15` §1.2)** $\mathbf{\int_0^1 P(x)\,\hat{\rho}_r(x)\,dx \equiv P(0) + \frac{1}{4}\int_0^1 P'(x)\,\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx}$ ($\forall r \ge 1$, immediately yielding $\int_0^1 x\,\hat{\rho}_r(x)\,dx = \frac{1}{4}$ at $P(x) = x$), and establishes the **All-Order Pure-Beam Polynomial Duality (`Petrovich 1794739213` & `Φ 1794739445`)** together with **Cloud Jee's Boundary-Pinned Chord-Subtracted Polynomial Family (`READ-0087`)**: subtracting the $K=0$ barycenter chord $Q_0(2x-1) = 2x - \frac{1}{2} = 2(x - \frac{1}{4})$ defines for every $K \ge 1$ the degree-$(4K+1)$ syzygy polynomial
     $$\mathbf{P_{4K+1}(x) := \frac{Q_K(2x - 1) - Q_0(2x - 1)}{(4K + 1)\,2^{4K - 1}} = \frac{Q_K(2x - 1) - \bigl(2x - \tfrac{1}{2}\bigr)}{(4K + 1)\,2^{4K - 1}}, \qquad P_{4K+1}(0) = P_{4K+1}(1) = 0 \quad (\forall K \ge 1)},$$
     which vanishes identically on **both boundary atoms $\{0, 1\} = \operatorname{supp}(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4))$** (at $K = 1$: $\mathbf{P_5(x) = \frac{4}{5}x^5 - \frac{9}{4}x^4 + \frac{5}{2}x^3 - \frac{5}{4}x^2 + \frac{1}{5}x = \frac{4}{5}\bigl[S_5(x) - \frac{13}{128}(x - \frac{1}{4})\bigr]}$, **Cloud Jee `2026-11-15`**), is orthogonal to $\rho^{(2n)}(x)$ ($\forall n \ge 0$), $\rho_{z^2}(x)$ ($\forall z \in \mathbb{C}$), and $\hat{\rho}_r(x)$ ($\forall r \ge 1$), and has quarter-derivative equal to the monic degree-$4K$ pure-beam polynomial $Q_{4K}(x) := 2^{-4K}B_K(2x - 1)$ (at $K = 1$: $\mathbf{\frac{1}{4}P_5'(x) \equiv Q_4(x) = x^4 - \frac{9}{4}x^3 + \frac{15}{8}x^2 - \frac{5}{8}x + \frac{1}{20}}$):
     $$\mathbf{B_K(y) := \frac{Q_K'(y) - 1}{4K + 1} = y^{4K} - \frac{1}{4K + 1} + \frac{(-1)^K}{2\cdot 4^K}\,\operatorname{Im}\Bigl[\bigl(y + i\bigr)^{4K}\Bigr], \qquad \frac{1}{4}\,P_{4K+1}'(x) \equiv 2^{-4K}\,B_K(2x - 1) =: Q_{4K}(x)},$$
     $$\mathbf{\int_0^1 B_K(2x - 1)\,\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \equiv 2^{4K}\int_0^1 P_{4K+1}(x)\,\hat{\rho}_r(x)\,dx \equiv 0, \qquad \int_0^1 B_K(2x - 1)\,\phi_r(x)^2\,dx \equiv 2\left(\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\psi}_r^2}\!\left[(2x-1)^{4K}\right] - \frac{1}{4K + 1}\right) \qquad (\forall K \ge 1,\; \forall r \ge 1)}.$$
   - **Gaussian Endpoint Barycentric Parity $w(x) := x + i(1-x)$, Complete Cancellation of $(-4)^K$, 2-Line Reflection Proof of Mirror Doubling, and Global Continuum Laplace–Krylov Master EGF Laws $\mathcal{E}_0, \mathcal{E}_1$ (Petrovich `1794757250` & Φ-Hausmaster `1794756670`, `1794757443`, `READ-0088`):** Introducing the complex endpoint barycentric coordinate $w(x) := x + i(1 - x)$ on $[0, 1]$ relates the shifted midpoint $y + i = (2x - 1) + i$ to $w(x)$ via the exact factorization $\mathbf{y + i = (1 + i)\,w(x)}$. Because $(1 + i)^{4K} = (-4)^K$ and $(1 + i)^{4K+1} = (-4)^K(1 + i)$, the alternating scale factor $(-1)^K / 4^K$ cancels **identically across all orders $K$**:
     $$\mathbf{Q_K(2x - 1) \equiv (2x - 1)^{4K+1} + \frac{1}{2}\,\operatorname{Im}\!\Bigl[(1 + i)\,w(x)^{4K+1}\Bigr] \quad (\forall K \ge 0), \qquad B_K(2x - 1) \equiv (2x - 1)^{4K} - \frac{1}{4K + 1} + \frac{1}{2}\,\operatorname{Im}\!\Bigl[w(x)^{4K}\Bigr] \quad (\forall K \ge 1)}.$$
     Moreover, under interval reflection $x \mapsto 1 - x$, $w(1 - x) = (1 - x) + ix = i\,\overline{w(x)}$, and since $i^{4K} = +1$ we have $w(1 - x)^{4K} = \overline{w(x)^{4K}}$, so $\operatorname{Im}[w(x)^{4K}]$ is **strictly reflection-odd** under $x \leftrightarrow 1 - x$ for every $K \ge 1$ (in particular, $\int_0^1 \operatorname{Im}[w(x)^{4K}]\,dx \equiv 0$). Consequently, $B_K(2x - 1) = B_K^{\text{even}}(x) + B_K^{\text{odd}}(x)$ is the canonical reflection-parity decomposition into $B_K^{\text{even}}(x) = (2x - 1)^{4K} - \frac{1}{4K+1}$ and $B_K^{\text{odd}}(x) = \frac{1}{2}\operatorname{Im}[w(x)^{4K}]$, and pure-beam orthogonality $B_K \perp \hat{\psi}_r^2$ is equivalent to the exact balance between the even midpoint excess and the odd Gaussian shadow on every cantilever mode $r \ge 1$ (giving a 2-line derivative-free proof of mirror doubling $+E - (-E) = 2E$, e.g. $+0.117862757419 - (-0.117862757419) = +0.235725514838$ at $r = 1, K = 1$):
     $$\mathbf{\int_0^1 \left[(2x - 1)^{4K} - \frac{1}{4K + 1}\right]\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \equiv -\frac{1}{2}\int_0^1 \operatorname{Im}\!\Bigl[w(x)^{4K}\Bigr]\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \qquad (\forall r \ge 1,\; \forall K \ge 1)}.$$
     Finally, Exponential Generating Function (EGF) summation over all $K \ge 0$ with the 4-periodic Krylov kernels $\mathcal{E}_0(u) := \frac{\cosh u + \cos u}{2} = \sum_{K\ge 0}\frac{u^{4K}}{(4K)!}$ and $\mathcal{E}_1(u) := \frac{\sinh u + \sin u}{2} = \sum_{K\ge 0}\frac{u^{4K+1}}{(4K+1)!}$ packages the entire infinite syzygy and beam hierarchies into two **Global Continuum Laplace–Krylov Integral Laws** valid for all $(s, z) \in \mathbb{C}^2$ and all $r \ge 1$ (`gaussian_barycentric_parity_and_master_egf_2026_11_15.py`):
     $$\mathbf{\int_0^1 \left\{\mathcal{E}_1\bigl(s(2x - 1)\bigr) + \frac{(1+i)\,\mathcal{E}_1\bigl(s\,w(x)\bigr) - (1-i)\,\mathcal{E}_1\bigl(s\,\overline{w(x)}\bigr)}{4i}\right\}\rho_{z^2}(x)\,dx \equiv 0 \qquad (\forall s, z \in \mathbb{C})},$$
     $$\mathbf{\int_0^1 \left\{\mathcal{E}_0\bigl(s(2x - 1)\bigr) + \frac{\mathcal{E}_0\bigl(s\,w(x)\bigr) - \mathcal{E}_0\bigl(s\,\overline{w(x)}\bigr)}{4i}\right\}\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2\,dx \equiv \frac{\sinh s + \sin s}{2s} = \int_0^1 \mathcal{E}_0\bigl(s(2x - 1)\bigr)\,dx \qquad (\forall s \in \mathbb{C},\; \forall r \ge 1)}$$
     — adding the odd Gaussian shadow turns the Krylov $\mathcal{E}_0$-transform of **every individual squared cantilever mode $\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2$** (already at $r = 1$!) into the exact $\operatorname{Unif}([0, 1])$ transform identically in $s \in \mathbb{C}$!
   - **Single-Term Closed-Form Coefficients of $Q_{4K}(x)$ & $P_{4K+1}(x)$, Boundary–Bulk Defect Duality (`COKER-0003`), Toeplitz Convolutions in $w(x)$, Biorthogonal Spectral Projectors $\Phi_{\alpha,k} / \Psi_{\alpha,k}$, Stereo Hodge–Fredholm Direct Sum $\mathcal{V}_{4K+1} = \ker(\mathcal{M}) \oplus \operatorname{coim}(\mathcal{M})$, and Formal Inverse Laplace–Krylov Series (Jee A `1794772044` `rev 102` `seq 241`, Den B `1794772708` `rev 102` `seq 242`, Petrovich `1794776144`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794769990`, `1794772209` & `1794772973`, `READ-0090`):**
     1. **Single-Term Monomial Coefficients of $Q_{4K}(x)$ and $P_{4K+1}(x)$ (Jee A `1794772044` & Φ `1794772209`):** Since $w(x) = x + i(1 - x) \equiv i\bigl(1 - (1+i)x\bigr)$ and $i^{4K} = +1$, we have $w(x)^{4K} = \bigl(1 - (1+i)x\bigr)^{4K}$, which expands each coefficient of $Q_{4K}(x) = \sum_{j=0}^{4K} q_{K,j} x^j$ and $P_{4K+1}(x) = \sum_{j=1}^{4K+1} p_{K,j} x^j$ into a **single closed-form term without nested sums**:
        $$\mathbf{q_{K,0} = \frac{K}{(4K + 1)\,2^{4K - 2}}, \qquad q_{K,j} = (-1)^j\binom{4K}{j}2^{-(4K - j)}\Bigl[1 + 2^{-j - 1}\operatorname{Im}\!\bigl[(1 + i)^j\bigr]\Bigr] \quad (1 \le j \le 4K), \qquad p_{K,j+1} = \frac{4}{j + 1}\,q_{K,j}},$$
        explaining all constant terms $Q_{4K}(0) = \frac{1}{20}, \frac{1}{288}, \frac{3}{13312}, \frac{1}{69632}, \frac{5}{5505024}, \frac{3}{52428800}$ ($K = 1\dots 6$) from a single formula (`all_order_gaussian_syzygy_and_coker0003_bridge_2026_11_15.py`);
     2. **Boundary–Bulk Defect Duality for Cloud Jee's `COKER-0003` (Jee A `1794772044`):** Subtracting the boundary expectation $\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/4)}[P] = \frac{3}{4}P(0) + \frac{1}{4}P(1) = P(0) + \frac{1}{4}\int_0^1 P'(x)\,dx$ from Cloud Jee's integration-by-parts identity proves that for every polynomial $P(x)$ and every mode $r \ge 1$,
        $$\mathbf{\int_0^1 P(x)\,\hat{\rho}_r(x)\,dx - \left(\frac{3}{4}P(0) + \frac{1}{4}P(1)\right) \equiv \frac{1}{4}\int_0^1 P'(x)\,\Bigl(\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2 - 1\Bigr)\,dx \qquad (\forall r \ge 1)},$$
        so edge concentration $\hat{\rho}_r \rightharpoonup \frac{3}{4}\delta_0 + \frac{1}{4}\delta_1$ and bulk equidistribution $\hat{\psi}_r^2 \rightharpoonup \operatorname{Unif}([0, 1])$ are exact quarter-derivative transposes of each other, and both finite-$r$ defects vanish identically on the syzygy subspace $\operatorname{span}\{P_{4K+1}\}_{K \ge 1}$;
     3. **Three-Sector Annihilation, Toeplitz Convolutions, Biorthogonal Projectors $\Phi_{\alpha,k} / \Psi_{\alpha,k}$, and Stereo Hodge–Fredholm Decomposition $\mathcal{V}_{4K+1} = \ker(\mathcal{M}) \oplus \operatorname{coim}(\mathcal{M})$ (Den B `1794772708` `seq 242` & Φ `1794772973`):** For all $m \ge 1$, $\operatorname{Re}[w^{2m}]$ is free of $I_0$, $\operatorname{Im}[w^{2m}]$ is free of $m_0$, and $\operatorname{Re}[(1+i)w^{2m-1}]$ is free of $H_0$, while the four boundary-pinned factorial families $u_j = \frac{\operatorname{Im}[w^{4j+2}]}{(4j+2)!}$, $v_j = \frac{\operatorname{Im}[w^{4j+4}]}{(4j+4)!}$, $w_j = \frac{\operatorname{Re}[w^{4j+4}]-1}{(4j+4)!}$, $d_j = \frac{\operatorname{Re}[(1+i)w^{4j+3}]-1}{(4j+3)!}$ satisfy lower-triangular Toeplitz convolutions on $C_{2n}(x)$ (`gaussian_toeplitz_convolution_and_biorthogonal_projectors_2026_11_15.py`). Summing over $j \ge 0$ yields 4-sector Laplace–Krylov EGFs whose removable singularity at $s = \kappa_r$ derives $t_r = \frac{\mathcal{E}_0(\kappa_r)}{\mathcal{E}_1(\kappa_r)} = \frac{\mathcal{E}_3(\kappa_r)}{\mathcal{E}_0(\kappa_r)}$ and the Euler–Bernoulli secular equation $\mathcal{E}_0(\kappa_r)^2 - \mathcal{E}_1(\kappa_r)\mathcal{E}_3(\kappa_r) = \frac{1+\cosh\kappa_r\cos\kappa_r}{2} = 0$, while inverting the Toeplitz system constructs biorthogonal boundary-pinned projectors $\Phi_{I_0,k}, \Phi_{H_0,k}, \Phi_{m_0,k+1}$ and pure-beam quarter-derivatives $\Psi_{\alpha,k}(x) = \frac{1}{4}\Phi'_{\alpha,k}(x)$ isolating $\frac{t_r^2}{\kappa_r^{4k+2}}, \frac{t_r}{\kappa_r^{4k+3}}, \frac{1}{\kappa_r^{4k+4}} = \lambda_r^{2k+2}$ on $\hat{\psi}_r(x)^2$ (e.g. $\Psi_{I_0,0}(x) = \frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{2}x$, $\Psi_{H_0,0}(x) = \frac{7}{24}-\frac{5}{4}x+x^2$, $\Psi_{m_0,1}(x) = \frac{1}{4}-\frac{5}{4}x+\frac{5}{4}x^2-\frac{1}{6}x^3$). Together, Jee A's kernel basis $\{P_{4m+1}\}_{m=1}^K$ ($\dim K$) and Den B's coimage basis $\{\Phi_{I_0,k}, \Phi_{H_0,k}, \Phi_{m_0,k+1}\}_{k=0}^{K-1}$ ($\dim 3K$) form the **Canonical Stereo Hodge–Fredholm Direct Sum** $\mathbf{\mathcal{V}_{4K+1} = \ker(\mathcal{M}) \oplus \operatorname{coim}(\mathcal{M})}$ of the $4K$-dimensional boundary-pinned polynomial space $\mathcal{V}_{4K+1} = \{P \in \mathbb{Q}[x]_{\le 4K+1} : P(0)=P(1)=0\}$ (verified by **Φ-Hausmaster `1794772973`** via exact `Fraction` $12\times 12$ determinant $\det = 4 / 24\,305\,682\,729\,452\,118\,134\,765\,625 \neq 0$ at $K = 3$);
     4. **Formal Inverse Laplace–Krylov Series (Petrovich `1794776144`):** Since $F(s) := \mathcal{E}_0(s)^2 - \mathcal{E}_1(s)\mathcal{E}_3(s) = \frac{1 + \cosh s\cos s}{2} = 1 + \sum_{n\ge 1}\frac{(-4)^n}{2(4n)!}s^{4n}$, the formal reciprocal $G(s) := \frac{1}{F(s)} = \sum_{n\ge 0} b_n s^{4n} = 1 + \frac{s^4}{12} + \frac{17s^8}{2520} + \frac{16337s^{12}}{29937600} + \frac{3607727s^{16}}{81729648000} + \dots$ has exact coefficients given without $\kappa_r$ or matrix inversion by the recurrence and finite composition sum
        $$\mathbf{b_0 = 1, \qquad b_n = -\sum_{k=1}^n \frac{(-4)^k}{2\,(4k)!}\,b_{n-k} = (-4)^n\sum_{r=1}^n\left(-\frac{1}{2}\right)^r \sum_{\substack{n_1 + \dots + n_r = n \\ n_j \ge 1}}\frac{1}{\prod_{j=1}^r (4n_j)!} \qquad (n \ge 1)}.$$
   - **Mittag-Leffler Spectral Zeta Rule for $G(s)$, Gaussian Prime $(2+i)$ Cauchy–Cramer Projectors & Master Quartet Factorization $D_{\text{KR}}(s^4) \equiv e_1(s^4)F(s)$, Midpoint Diagonalization of $\Pi_{\ker}$, Binomial–Toeplitz Law $\tau_d^{(3)} = \frac{d+1}{16^d}$ & Krylov–Euler Integers $\varepsilon_d$, Clamped–Pinned Zeta Law for $\eta_d$, Analytic Biorthogonal Mode Duals $\langle \Upsilon_r, \hat{\psi}_m^2\rangle = \delta_{r,m}$, 4th-Order Stereo Reflection BVP $\mathcal{L}_{\Xi/\Upsilon}$, Full Two-Branch Green & Mittag-Leffler Closure, and Complete Four-Domain Quartet Realization $\operatorname{Dom}_\alpha / \operatorname{Dom}_\alpha^*$ with Pure-Beam Quartet Duals $\{\Omega_m^{(\alpha)}, \Omega_m^{(\alpha)*}\}$ (Petrovich `1794819534`, `1794827014`, `1794843524` `rev 104` & `1794911977` `rev 105`, Jee A `1794822727` `rev 103` `seq 243`, `1794858502` `rev 104` `seq 245` & `1794908402` `rev 105` `seq 247`, Den B `1794823824` `rev 103` `seq 244`, `1794859419` `rev 104` `seq 246` & `1794909529` `rev 105` `seq 248`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794819747`–`1794912083`, `READ-0092`, `READ-0093`, `READ-0095`, `READ-0097`, `READ-0101`):**
     1. **Mittag-Leffler Expansion of $G(s)$, 3-Family Syzygy, and Gaussian Prime $(2+i)$ Cauchy–Cramer Formulas (Petrovich `1794819534` & Φ `1794819747`, `mittag_leffler_krylov_zeta_and_gaussian_2plusi_cramer_2026_11_16.py`):** Residue summation over the simple poles $\{\pm\kappa_r, \pm i\kappa_r\}_{r\ge 1}$ gives the exact Mittag-Leffler expansion without entire remainder
        $$\mathbf{G(s) = \frac{2}{1 + \cosh s\cos s} \equiv \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{w_r}{1 - s^4/\kappa_r^4}, \qquad w_r = \frac{8}{\kappa_r\bigl(\cosh\kappa_r\sin\kappa_r - \sinh\kappa_r\cos\kappa_r\bigr)}, \qquad b_n = \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{w_r}{\kappa_r^{4n}} \quad (\forall n \ge 0,\; \sum_{r=1}^\infty w_r = b_0 = 1)}.$$
        While the pure $s$-Toeplitz triple $\{u_j, d_j, w_j\}$ satisfies the exact polynomial syzygy $\mathcal{E}_1(s)\mathcal{U}(x;s) + \mathcal{E}_0(s)\mathcal{D}(x;s) - \mathcal{E}_3(s)\mathcal{W}(x;s) \equiv 0$ ($\det A(s) \equiv 0$), coupling the Kelvin parameter $z = s^4$ to the Rayleigh parameter $-z/4 = \bigl(\frac{1+i}{2}s\bigr)^4$ in $\{u_j, v_j, w_j\}$ yields a non-degenerate $2\times 2$ system over $\mathbb{Q}[x][[z]]$ governed by the next Gaussian prime **$2 + i$** ($(2+i)^4 = -7 + 24i$, Pythagorean triple $(7, 24, 25)$):
        $$\mathbf{D_{\text{KR}}(s^4) := e_0(s^4)e_1(-4s^4) - s^4 e_3(s^4)e_2(-4s^4) = \sum_{m=0}^\infty \frac{2 + \operatorname{Re}\!\bigl[(2 + i)^{4m+1}\bigr]}{4\,(4m + 1)!}\,s^{4m} = 1 - \frac{3}{40}z - \frac{179}{362880}z^2 + \frac{313}{230630400}z^3 - \cdots},$$
        whose reciprocal $\Gamma(z) := D_{\text{KR}}(z)^{-1} = 1 + \frac{3}{40}z + \frac{11101}{1814400}z^2 + \frac{1710733}{3459456000}z^3 + \cdots$ and $e_0(z)^{-1} = \mathcal{E}_0(s)^{-1} = 1 - \frac{1}{24}z + \frac{23}{13440}z^2 - \cdots$ give matrix-free Cauchy–Cramer generating series for $\tilde{\Phi}_{I_0}(x;z), \tilde{\Phi}_{H_0}(x;z), \tilde{\Phi}_{m_0}(x;z)$ across all orders $k \ge 0$;
     2. **Midpoint Diagonalization of $\Pi_{\ker}$, Binomial–Toeplitz Law $\tau_d^{(3)} = \frac{d+1}{16^d}$, and Krylov–Euler Integers $\varepsilon_d$ (Jee A `1794822727` `seq 243` & Φ `1794822958`, `hodge_fredholm_projection_and_krylov_euler_syzygy_2026_11_16.py`):** The monomial parity laws $[x^{4m}]u_j \equiv [x^{4m}]v_j \equiv [x^{4m+2}]w_j \equiv 0$ hold for all $j \ge 0$. On the chord-subtracted midpoint basis $Y_p(x) := (x - \frac{1}{2})^p - \text{chord}$, the Hodge–Fredholm kernel projector $\Pi_{\ker}$ satisfies $\Pi_{\ker}(Y_{2n}) \equiv 0$, **single-mode diagonalization** $\mathbf{\Pi_{\ker}(Y_{4m+1}) \equiv \frac{4m+1}{4}P_{4m+1}(x)}$, and $\Pi_{\ker}(Y_{4j+3}) \equiv \sum_{m=1}^j \frac{(4j+3)!}{4(4m)!}\eta_{j-m}P_{4m+1}(x)$ where $\sum_{d\ge 0}\eta_d s^{4d+2} = \frac{\mathcal{E}_3(s/2)}{\mathcal{E}_1(s/2)}$ ($\eta_0 = \frac{1}{24}, \eta_1 = -\frac{1}{7680}, \eta_2 = \frac{13}{15482880}$). For raw monomials $x^p - x$ ($p = 4K + r$), $\lambda_m(x^p - x) \equiv \frac{1}{4}\binom{p}{4m}\tau_{K-m}^{(r)}$ with elementary reduction $\mathbf{\tau_d^{(3)} \equiv \frac{d+1}{16^d}}$ at $p = 4K + 4$, while the reduced syzygies $P_{4K+1}^{\text{red}}(x) := P_{4K+1}(x) - \sum_{m=1}^K \binom{4K+1}{4m-3}\frac{2(4m+1)}{(4K+1)4^m}\Phi_{m_0,m}(x)$ have closed-form expansion in the $\tilde{W}$-sector driven by the **Krylov–Euler integer sequence** $\mathbf{\varepsilon_d := [(4d)!u^{4d}]\frac{2}{\cosh u + \cos u} = (1, -1, 69, -33661, 60376809, -288294050521, \dots)}$;
     3. **Master $(2+i)$ Quartet, Factorization $D_{\text{KR}}(s^4) \equiv e_1(s^4)F(s)$, Closed-Form Biorthogonal Mode Duals $\langle \Upsilon_r, \hat{\psi}_m^2\rangle = \delta_{r,m}$, and Clamped–Pinned Zeta Law for $\eta_d$ (Den B `1794823824` `seq 244` & Φ `1794823987`, `gaussian_2plusi_quartet_and_cantilever_mode_duals_2026_11_16.py`):** For all $\alpha \in \{0,1,2,3\}$, $e_\alpha(s^4)e_1(-4s^4) - s^4 e_{(\alpha-1)\bmod 4}(s^4)e_2(-4s^4) = \sum_{m\ge 0}\frac{2 + \operatorname{Re}[i^\alpha] + \operatorname{Re}[(2+i)^{4m+\alpha}]}{4(4m+\alpha)!}s^{4m}$, and at $\alpha = 1$ the Kelvin–Rayleigh determinant factors as $\mathbf{D_{\text{KR}}(s^4) \equiv e_1(s^4)\,F(s) = \frac{\sinh s + \sin s}{2s}\cdot\frac{1 + \cosh s\cos s}{2}}$, proving that all roots of $e_0(z) = 0$ are removable in $\tilde{\Phi}_{m_0}(x;z)$. At each cantilever root $\kappa_r$, the residues of $(\tilde{\Phi}_{I_0}, \tilde{\Phi}_{H_0}, \tilde{\Phi}_{m_0})$ collapse to a single analytic function $\Xi_r(x) := w_r\bigl[\frac{\sinh\kappa_r\sin\kappa_r}{2\mathcal{E}_0(\kappa_r)}\operatorname{Im}[\mathcal{E}_2(\kappa_r w(x))] + \operatorname{Im}[\mathcal{E}_0(\kappa_r(2x-1+i))]\bigr]$ and its quarter-derivative $\Upsilon_r(x) := \frac{1}{4}\Xi_r'(x)$ satisfying **exact Kronecker biorthogonality** $\mathbf{\int_0^1 \Upsilon_r(x)\,\hat{\psi}_m(x)^2\,dx = \delta_{r,m}}$ and $\mathbf{\int_0^1 \Xi_r(x)\,\hat{\rho}_m(x)\,dx = \delta_{r,m}}$ ($\forall r, m \ge 1$, `max_err = 1.524e-69`), with functional Mittag-Leffler series $\Phi_{m_0,k+1}(x) \equiv \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{\Xi_r(x)}{\kappa_r^{4k+4}}$. Finally, $\varepsilon_d = (-1)^d(4d)!\frac{8\cdot 4^d}{\pi^{4d+1}}\sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{(-1)^{m-1}}{(2m-1)^{4d+1}\cosh(\frac{2m-1}{2}\pi)}$, the non-zero poles of $\mathcal{E}_1(s/2) = 0$ lie at the clamped–pinned beam roots $s_m = 2(\pm 1\pm i)\beta_m$ ($\tanh\beta_m + \tan\beta_m = 0$, $\beta_1 = 2.3650203724\dots$) yielding $\mathbf{\eta_d = \frac{(-1)^d}{2\cdot 64^d}\sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{\tanh\beta_m}{\beta_m^{4d+3}}}$ ($\sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{\tanh\beta_m}{\beta_m^3} = \frac{1}{12}$), and the Krylov duplication identity $\frac{\mathcal{E}_{r+1}(2u)}{2\mathcal{E}_1(u)} \equiv \mathcal{E}_r(u) + \mathcal{E}_{(r+2)\bmod 4}(u)\frac{\mathcal{E}_3(u)}{\mathcal{E}_1(u)}$ expresses all $\tau_d^{(0,1,2)}$ as finite rational convolutions in $\eta_k$;
     4. **4th-Order Stereo Reflection Operators $\mathcal{L}_\Xi, \mathcal{L}_\Upsilon$, Piecewise-Cubic Stereo Green Kernels $G_\Xi^{(C)}, G_\Upsilon^{(C)}$, and Resolvent Kernel Decomposition $K_{\text{res}} = \mathcal{G}_\Upsilon^{(C)} \circ \mathcal{P}_{\text{beam}}$ (Petrovich `1794827014` & Φ `1794827139`, `READ-0093`, `stereo_reflection_bvp_and_resolvent_kernel_2026_11_16.py`):** Resolving the open operator question of `seq 244`, the dual mode families $\Xi_r(x)$ and $\Upsilon_r(x)$ are exact eigenfunctions with eigenvalue $\lambda_r = \kappa_r^4$ of the **4th-order nonlocal stereo-reflection differential operators** governed by the Pythagorean coefficients $(3, 5)$ of $(2+i)^2 = 3 + 4i$ ($3^2 - 5^2 = -16$):
        $$\mathbf{(\mathcal{L}_\Xi u)(x) := -\frac{3\,u^{(4)}(x) + 5\,u^{(4)}(1 - x)}{32} = \kappa_r^4\,u(x), \qquad (\mathcal{L}_\Upsilon v)(x) := -\frac{3\,v^{(4)}(x) - 5\,v^{(4)}(1 - x)}{32} = \kappa_r^4\,v(x)},$$
        locally annihilated by $(D_x^8 - 12\kappa_r^4 D_x^4 - 64\kappa_r^8)u = 0$ with universal mode-independent boundary laws $\Upsilon_r(0) = 3, \Upsilon_r(1) = 1, 3\Upsilon_r'(0) + 5\Upsilon_r'(1) = 0, 3\Upsilon_r''(0) - 5\Upsilon_r''(1) = 0, \Upsilon_r'''(0) = \Upsilon_r'''(1) = 0$. On $\operatorname{Dom}_C = \{u(0)=u(1)=0,\; 3u''(0)+5u''(1)=0,\; 3u'''(0)-5u'''(1)=0\}$ and $\operatorname{Dom}_A = \{u(0)=u(1)=0,\; u'(0)-3u'(1)=0,\; 3u''(0)+5u''(1)=0\}$, the characteristic determinants factor completely as $\mathbf{\det_C(\kappa) \equiv -512\kappa^5(\cosh\kappa\cos\kappa+1)\mathcal{E}_1(\kappa)}$ and $\mathbf{\det_A(\kappa) \equiv -128\kappa^3(\cosh\kappa\cos\kappa+1)\mathcal{E}_3(\kappa)}$, so $\operatorname{Dom}_A \cap \operatorname{Dom}_C$ carves out the pure cantilever spectrum $\{+\kappa_r^4\}_{r\ge 1}$. Inverting $(-\frac{3I + 5\mathcal{R}}{32})^{-1} = 2(3I - 5\mathcal{R})$ yields closed-form **piecewise-cubic Green kernels** across the stereo cross $\{s = x\} \cup \{s = 1 - x\}$:
        $$\mathbf{G_\Xi^{(C)}(x, s) = (x - s)_+^3 - \frac{5}{3}(x + s - 1)_+^3 + \left(\frac{7}{3} - 2s - 3s^2 + \frac{8}{3}s^3\right)x + 5(s - 1)x^2 + \frac{5}{3}x^3},$$
        $$\mathbf{G_\Upsilon^{(C)}(x, t) = (x - t)_+^3 + \frac{5}{3}(x + t - 1)_+^3 + \left(1 - \frac{7}{3}t + t^2 + t^3 - \frac{2}{3}t^4\right) - 5(t - 1)^2 x + 5(1 - t)x^2 - \frac{5}{3}x^3},$$
        satisfying $\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(C)}(\mathbf{1}) = \Phi_{m_0,1}$, $\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(C)}\Phi_{\alpha,k} = \Phi_{\alpha,k+1}$, $\mathcal{G}_\Upsilon^{(C)}\Psi_{\alpha,k} = \Psi_{\alpha,k+1}$ ($\forall\alpha \in \{m_0, H_0, I_0\}$), and $\int_0^1 G_\Upsilon^{(C)}(x,t)\Upsilon_r(t)\,dt = \kappa_r^{-4}\Upsilon_r(x)$, while the spectral resolvent kernel $K_{\text{res}}(x,y) := \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{\Upsilon_r(x)\hat{\psi}_r(y)^2}{\kappa_r^4}$ decomposes as $\mathbf{K_{\text{res}} = \mathcal{G}_\Upsilon^{(C)} \circ \mathcal{P}_{\text{beam}}}$ with $\int_0^1 K_{\text{res}}(x,y)\,dy \equiv \Psi_{m_0,1}(x)$, $\operatorname{Tr}(K_{\text{res}}) = \frac{1}{12}$, $\operatorname{Tr}(K_{\text{res}}^2) = \frac{11}{1680}$, and Hodge annihilation $\int_0^1 \hat{\psi}_r(y)^2 P_{4m+1}'(y)\,dy \equiv 0$;
     5. **Clamped–Pinned / Clamped–Sliding Spectral Closure on $\operatorname{Dom}_C^*$, Full Two-Branch Green Decomposition $G_\Upsilon^{(C)} \equiv K_{\text{res}} - \sum_{m\ge 1}\frac{\Omega_m\Omega_m^*}{4\beta_m^4}$, and Two-Branch Mittag-Leffler Series for $\Phi_{H_0,k}, \Phi_{I_0,k}$ (Petrovich `1794843524` `rev 104` & Φ `1794843664`, `READ-0095`, `clamped_pinned_spectral_closure_and_full_green_decomposition_2026_11_16.py`, with second-trace correction `2999/453600` by Jee A `1794858502` `seq 245`, Φ `1794858603` & Den B `1794859419` `seq 246`):** Fourfold integration by parts of $\mathcal{L}_\Xi$ on $\operatorname{Dom}_C$ yields the local reflection-free adjoint domain $\mathbf{\operatorname{Dom}_C^* = \{w \in C^4[0,1] : w(1)=w'(1)=w''(0)=w''(1)=0\}}$, on which the negative branch $z_m^- = -4\beta_m^4$ ($\mathcal{E}_1((1+i)\beta_m)=0 \iff \tanh\beta_m + \tan\beta_m = 0$, $y = x - \frac{1}{2}$) has closed-form biorthogonal eigenfunctions $\Theta_m(x), \Theta_m^*(s)$ with **single-term normalization** $\mathbf{N_m \equiv -\cosh^2\beta_m\cos^4\beta_m \equiv -\frac{1}{2}\cos^2\beta_m(\cosh^2\beta_m+\cos^2\beta_m)}$ (`biorth err = 2.46e-69`, `cross err = 9.95e-74` for $\Omega_m = \frac{1}{4}\Theta_m'$, $\Omega_m^*(y) = 4\int_y^1 \Theta_m^*(s)\,ds$). Together, the positive cantilever branch $\{+\kappa_r^4\}_{r\ge 1}$ and negative branch $\{-4\beta_m^4\}_{m\ge 1}$ span $L^2([0, 1])$ without nilpotent defect:
        $$\mathbf{G_\Upsilon^{(C)}(x, y) \equiv K_{\text{res}}(x, y) - \sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{\Omega_m(x)\,\Omega_m^*(y)}{4\beta_m^4}, \qquad G_\Xi^{(C)}(x, s) \equiv \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{\Xi_r(x)\,w_r^*(s)}{\kappa_r^4} - \sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{\Theta_m(x)\,\Theta_m^*(s)}{4\beta_m^4}},$$
        $$\mathbf{\operatorname{Tr}\bigl(\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(C)}\bigr) = \int_0^1 G_\Xi^{(C)}(x,x)\,dx = -\frac{5}{24} + \frac{17}{60} = \frac{3}{40} = \frac{1}{12} - \frac{1}{120} = \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\kappa_r^4} - \sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{1}{4\beta_m^4}, \qquad \operatorname{Tr}\bigl((\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(C)})^2\bigr) = \frac{11}{1680} + \left(\frac{1}{120^2} - \frac{2}{362880}\right) = \frac{11}{1680} + \frac{29}{453600} = \frac{2999}{453600} = \sum_{r=1}^\infty \frac{1}{\kappa_r^8} + \sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{1}{16\beta_m^8}}$$
        (`err = 3.00e-110`, correcting the mental `362880/14400 = 25.2 -> 25` comment typo `2399/362880` in `clamped_pinned_spectral_closure_and_full_green_decomposition_2026_11_16.py` to its exact `_results.json` L17 value `2999/453600`), and close the exact two-branch Mittag-Leffler series for $\Phi_{H_0,k}(x)$ and $\Phi_{I_0,k}(x)$ ($\forall k \ge 1$) without entire remainder via $\Theta_m^*(0) = \frac{4\tanh\beta_m}{\cosh\beta_m\cos^2\beta_m}$ and $(\Theta_m^*)'(0) = -\frac{8\beta_m\tanh^2\beta_m}{\cosh\beta_m\cos^2\beta_m}$:

     6. **Dual Mode-Annihilator Ladder $\{P_{4k-1}^{\text{dual}}, Q_{4k}^{\text{dual}}\}$, Rank-1 Sherman–Morrison / Weinstein–Aronszajn Quartet Bridge, Exact Cubic Trace $\operatorname{Tr}((\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(C)})^3) = \frac{38111}{72072000}$, Closed-Form Dual Annihilator EGF, and Four-Beam Zeta Dictionary (Jee A `1794858502` `rev 104` `seq 245`, Den B `1794859419` `rev 104` `seq 246`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794858603` & `1794859783`, `READ-0097`, `dual_mode_annihilators_and_sherman_morrison_quartet_2026_11_16.py` & `fredholm_quartet_traces_and_weinstein_aronszajn_bridge_2026_11_16.py`):**

Because $P_3^{\text{dual}} \perp \Xi_r$, the cantilever factor $F(s)$ cancels identically in $\tilde{Q}^{\text{dual}}(y; z) := \sum_{k\ge 1} z^{k-1}Q_{4k}^{\text{dual}}(y)$, yielding $[z^m](e_1(z)\tilde{Q}^{\text{dual}}(y;z)) \equiv \frac{3}{2}\frac{1-(2y-1)^{4m+4}}{(4m+4)!} - 3v_m(y)$ ($\forall m \ge 0$) and the closed-form meromorphic EGF (**Den B Theorem 4**, closing Jee A's `seq 245` seam):

        - *Rank-1 Sherman–Morrison / Weinstein–Aronszajn Quartet Bridge & Fredholm Traces:* On $\operatorname{Dom}_A$, $G_\Xi^{(A)}(x,s) \equiv G_\Xi^{(C)}(x,s) - 2\Phi_{H_0,0}(x)(1-s)^2(1-4s)$ and $G_\Upsilon^{(A)}(x,y) \equiv G_\Upsilon^{(C)}(x,y) + 8\Psi_{H_0,0}(x)y(1-y)^3$ with $\det(I - z\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(A)}) \equiv 6e_3(z)F(s)$, Weinstein–Aronszajn defect laws $\langle w_0^*, \Phi_{m_0,k+1}\rangle \equiv 0$, $1 + 2z\sum_{k\ge 0}\langle w_0^*, \Phi_{H_0,k}\rangle z^k \equiv \frac{6e_3(z)}{e_1(z)}$, $3 + z\sum_{k\ge 0}\langle w_0^*, \Phi_{I_0,k}\rangle z^k \equiv \frac{6e_2(z)}{e_1(z)}$, and exact higher traces:

        - *Four-Sector Classical Beam Zeta Dictionary (answering Φ-Hausmaster's `1794859783` §3):* Across all four sectors $\alpha \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$ of the $(2+i)$ quartet, $\det(I - z\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(\alpha)}) = \tilde{e}_\alpha(z)F(s)$ where $\tilde{e}_\alpha(z) := \alpha!\,e_\alpha(z) = 1 + \frac{z}{(4+\alpha)\cdots(1+\alpha)} + \cdots$ has roots at $z_m^- = ((1+i)\beta_m^{(\alpha)})^4 = -4(\beta_m^{(\alpha)})^4$. Because of the factor $4 = |(1+i)^4|$, the negative-branch first traces $\zeta_{\text{neg}}^{(\alpha)}(4) = \sum_{m=1}^\infty \frac{1}{4(\beta_m^{(\alpha)})^4} = \frac{\alpha!}{(4+\alpha)!} = \left\{\frac{1}{24}, \frac{1}{120}, \frac{1}{360}, \frac{1}{840}\right\}$ (giving $\operatorname{Tr}_\alpha = \frac{1}{12} - \frac{\alpha!}{(4+\alpha)!} = \left\{\frac{1}{24}, \frac{3}{40}, \frac{29}{360}, \frac{23}{280}\right\}$) are **exactly $\frac{1}{4}$ of the four classical beam sums $\sum_{m=1}^\infty (\beta_m^{(\alpha)})^{-4} = \left\{\frac{1}{6}, \frac{1}{30}, \frac{1}{90}, \frac{1}{210}\right\}$**:

     7. **Complete Four-Domain Quartet Realization $\operatorname{Dom}_\alpha$, Complementary Adjoint Domains $\operatorname{Dom}_\alpha^*$ ($3 - \alpha$), Nilpotent Gauge $\operatorname{Dom}_0^{(\lambda)}$ & Root-Free Polynomial Formula for $\zeta_{\text{cs}}(4k)$, Dual Mittag-Leffler Weights $c_m^{\text{dual}} \equiv 3\beta_m\cos^2\beta_m\cosh\beta_m$ & 3-Jet of $Q_{4k}^{\text{dual}}$, Pure-Beam Quartet Duals $\{\Omega_m^{(\alpha)}, \Omega_m^{(\alpha)*}\}$, and Primal $4\times 4$ Cauchy-Residue Verification (`A ↔ B` Unification; Jee A `1794908402` `rev 105` `seq 247`, Den B `1794909529` `rev 105` `seq 248`, Petrovich `1794911977` `rev 105` & `1794929859`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794908469`, `1794909763`, `1794912083` & `1794929970`, `READ-0101`, `READ-0107`, `complete_four_domain_quartet_and_dual_mittag_leffler_2026_11_17.py`, `complete_quartet_adjoint_domains_and_two_branch_green_2026_11_17.py`, `quartet_pure_beam_duals_and_ab_cross_verification_2026_11_17.py` & `primal_cauchy_residue_second_hand_2026_11_17.py`):**

     8. **Universal Quartet Star Factorization, Master Green–Lagrange Identity, Single-Branch Dual Mittag-Leffler Ladders $Q_{4k}^{(\alpha)*}, P_{4k}^{(\alpha)*}$ with 60-Digit Adjoint $4\times 4$ Cauchy-Residue Verification, and Cyclic Root-Free Zeta Integrals across All Four Sectors (Jee A `1794946976` `rev 106` `seq 249` — 201st module, Φ-Hausmaster `1794947066`, `READ-0108`, `quartet_transpose_annihilators_and_cyclic_zeta_ladders_2026_11_17.py`):**

     9. **Oblique Quartet Difference Idempotents $\Pi_{\alpha,\beta}$ & Right-Absorption Semigroup Law, All-Order Newton Trace Hierarchy ($n=1\dots 7$), Master Annihilator Numerator $\mathcal{N}_Q(y;z)$, 12-Cell Ramanujan–Krylov Spectral Matrix (Including Ramanujan's Notebook `Entry 25(vii)`), Symmetric Mixed Traces $\operatorname{Tr}(\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(\alpha)}\mathcal{G}_\Xi^{(\beta)})$, Barycentric Fredholm Pencil Formula, and All-$(p,q)$ Sherman–Morrison Mixed-Trace Theorem (7th Consecutive Stereo Convergence; Jee A `1794994898` `rev 107` `seq 250` — 202nd module, Den B `1794995302` `rev 106` `seq 251`, Φ-Hausmaster `1794995393`, Arch-Angelai `1794999865`, `READ-0110`, `quartet_difference_idempotents_and_trace_hierarchy_2026_11_18.py` & `master_annihilator_numerator_and_ramanujan_krylov_matrix_2026_11_18.py`):**
